D 0127 D0116 Withdrawn Lead Generation Replanned From Founder Direction

D-0127. D-0116 is withdrawn

The decision

Hunter, 2026-09-05, verbatim: "remove D-0116."

D-0116 (2026-08-31) fixed the lead engine as social media first, MFC's own free online Leadership Community second, and being in rooms third. That ordering no longer stands. Lead generation is re-planned from the founder direction below, and nothing in D-0116 is carried forward by default.

The founder direction that replaces it, verbatim

  1. "Not our actual community - joining other communities and going to explain or simply teach mental fitness (reps)." — The rooms channel is walking into gatherings that already exist and teaching a rep in five to fifteen minutes. It is not founding a community.
  2. "180,000 followers is my friend Tom D (is interested) in being an ambassador - so the strategy is to incorporate an ambassador program."
  3. "make the strategy for our instagram having 8800 followers now, and a few hundred on linked In. I also have socials which i am not active on at the moment. And don't want to be too active - although open to it if evidence supports this move/approach."
  4. "If community build (our own) is the best approach or one of... then i am open to entertaining it. is it virtual, in real life? how do we reach people - perhaps track data etc?" — MFC's own community is an option to be evaluated, not the engine.
  5. "i can give five hours to lead gen a week - thats at this stage, could be more if we need to sell the services or less if its up and running and orgs and communities are reaching out."

What this does and does not change

  • D-0111 §3 is unaffected. MFC's own community remains not authorised for build. D-0127 authorises its evaluation inside the lead-generation plan, nothing more.
  • FINAL-03 is not reissued. The committee holds the 17 August paper. The reconciled position returns to them as part of the consolidated strategy, as D-0116 itself said it would.
  • The ambassador programme draft (90-meta/PROGRAMME-2026-08-25-ambassador-v1.html) and the Tom debrief stand as the starting point. Nothing about Tom is public and no agreement exists.
  • R1's finding — that personal-profile social posting is a trust asset rather than lead generation — is no longer overridden by a founder ordering. It is evidence the plan must weigh, which is what Hunter asked for: "open to it if evidence supports this move/approach."

Propagation

  • D-0116 frontmatter: status: superseded, superseded_by: D-0127. Done in this commit.
  • The marketing-leads topic record and Hub: updated when the plan lands, not before.

Original source: 30-decisions/D-0127-d0116-withdrawn-lead-generation-replanned-from-founder-direction.md

01 The Plan

Lead generation — the plan

Built against 00-DEFINITION-OF-DONE.md, which was frozen before this document existed. Governed by D-0127, which withdrew D-0116 and replaced it with Hunter's own five directions of 5 September.

PART ONE — THE ANSWER

⛔ READ THIS FIRST — five hours does not reach thirteen cohorts

The adversarial pass broke the frame that eight heads improved inside without ever weighing (§7.1). Both the heads' improvement round and their agreement round made the parts better and left one assumption unchallenged: that this fits in five hours and lands on thirteen cohorts. It does not, and Hunter is owed the real number rather than a table that sums to 5.00 because it was made to.

The plan as first written The honest position
Hours a week, steady state 5.00 ~6 to 7.3 — the range is what the two adversarial passes independently arrived at
Month one 5.00 ~10.9 — funded by the setup trigger added at §1.3 (was locked in the first draft)
Client organisations (P50) 4.0 ~3.0 on the two channels worth trusting
Cohorts 13 ~10
Minimum viable 13 13

Three things the 5.00 concealed, each verifiable inside the document:

  1. ⛔ Option D — the community option this plan actually recommends — costs 12 hours a year and appeared in the first draft's hours table as 0 — now costed at 0.25 in §1.3. The headline total is only true if Hunter declines the recommendation being made to him.
  2. ⛔ The one-off month-one builds are funded "from the flex ceiling", and the flex rule triggers only when a written proposal has been issued. In month one no proposal exists. The plan's own rule locked the mechanism meant to fund its own prerequisites — fixed: §1.3's flex rule now carries a setup trigger that fires in month one.
  3. ⛔ Roughly 65 hours a year of named work is in no table — the guest-first visits §2.4 says are how rooms 2, 3 and 18 are entered (~17.5h), the ambassador pack, agreement, Mindframe call and due diligence, the printed cards, the recorded rep, the MFM cohort message, the monthly one-page, and the founder questions.

And thirteen was only reached by counting three channels the plan itself disclaims — the ambassador programme ("counted honestly at nearly nothing"), social ("an entirely untested channel" whose own §4.1 evidence review concluded no), and community rooms ("the plan never again reports a community room as a lead source"). Those three cost 40% of the budget for 25% of the target, from the sources the document says not to trust. Remove them and the plan misses; keep them and the hours do not fit.

The choice is Hunter's, and it is FOUNDER QUESTION 11. Three real forks:

(a) Accept ~10 cohorts and a sub-minimum-viable year one — the plan below, run at five hours, honestly labelled. (b) Give seven to eight hours — the plan below reaches ~13. (c) Change the mix or the price — 15-THE-MONEY-CASE §2 is where that argument lives, not here.

Recommendation: (a) for the first quarter, then decide with real data. Months 1–3 are not expected to produce bookings at any conversion rate; they are expected to produce the first real value of X4, which is the number every fork depends on. Choosing between (a), (b) and (c) in December on three months of MFC's own numbers is a better decision than choosing today on assumptions.

Everything below is the plan. It is good work and the heads improved it substantially. It is presented at five hours because that is what Hunter offered — with the honest total marked at §1.3 and the shortfall marked here, rather than buried.

What MFC does, in the order of what it returns for what it costs

⚑ This replaces "the week, as a calendar." Hunter, 2026-09-05: "i don't need a week by week breakdown of what i need to do." There is no day-of-week allocation anywhere in this document. The hours are a total and a ceiling, not a timetable. What follows is the order of return, which is not the same thing as an order of doing.

⚑ Restructured after the heads' improvement pass. The first draft modelled thirteen separate transactions; it now models four client organisations. 15-THE-MONEY-CASE §3 had already ruled that way and this plan had not picked it up: "Forty-two engagements sold one at a time is a fantasy; sold as seven to nine client organisations averaging five cohorts each, it is a sales job one person can do." Thirteen Foundations at ten people is 130 participants — four to five employers, not thirteen strangers. That single change is what makes the room count fit inside five hours.

The working year is ~44 weeks (§1.3 — the Australian December–January dead zone is real). Margin per cohort above cost is $2,942.82 at ten people (15-THE-MONEY-CASE §2); at 3.25 cohorts per client organisation that is ~$9,564 per client. The hours below are the honest 6.30 steady state, not the 5.00 the front page calls fiction — ranking on the underfunded column would manufacture a productivity figure out of a row §1.3 itself says was plugged rather than costed.

⚑ J1 — every channel is now re-derived from a primary segment, 2026-09-05

This plan was built before MFC had a segment definition. It now has a RECOMMENDED one, not an adopted one: [[60-projects/lead-generation-v3/04-THE-STRATEGY|04-THE-STRATEGY.md]] — purpose-led small-to-medium employers, roughly 20–200 staff, Sydney, founder/CEO/MD reachable, at 6/10 confidence on the proposition "this is the right segment to try first."

⛔ The choice is Hunter's alone (AG-085, DoD §6) and he has not made it. The edits below operationalise a recommendation, and if he chooses differently they are re-derived, not defended. (Added 2026-09-05 after the adversarial pass, which correctly found the first version of this note asserting "it now has one" as though the decision had been taken — the one thing the definition of done reserved to him.) ⚠ And the lower bound of the band is contested: D-0121 (2026-09-03, Hunter) retired all three minimum rules, putting a Foundations cohort at 3–20 with no minimum, so the floor could be as low as ~15. See 04-THE-STRATEGY.md §"Who MFC is for". Everything below is unchanged except where marked.

What re-deriving found, and it is uncomfortable:

  • Partnerships (row 1) is the only channel that demonstrably reaches the primary segment. R1 Part 6 Route 2 names the doors with phone numbers — CCWT (02) 9281 8822, NDS, NCOSS, Homelessness NSW, Ageing Australia — costed at "10–14 hours initial; 2–3 hours/week." It is now the spine of this plan, and its hours 40–55 remain named, unfunded work.
  • ⛔ Rooms — professional (part of row 4) is UNVERIFIED against the primary segment. Eleven of the sixteen professional rooms are chambers and Rotary clubs, dominated in Australia by owner-operators and micro-businesses — the segment the strategy ranks fourth, not the primary. §1.1's own X6 note already flags this and does not answer it. Nothing in the vault verifies that a single 20–200-staff purpose-led employer's CEO or People lead attends any of these rooms. Not dropped: room 29 (Vistage) has verified purchasing authority in the room and room 1 (IWR) publishes a speaker route. But the 1.0 client is an assumption about who is in the room, and it is checkable on the first two visits — see the room-log change at §6.1.
  • ⚑ Rooms — community (the rest of row 4) reaches no buyer in any segment and moves out of this plan into a separate mission line. This plan already counted nine of them at zero doors. See §2.2.
  • Social (row 2) is unchanged and its purpose is now stated once: the verification and retention layer beneath every other channel, not a source of leads. Cadence stays at the floor.
  • Ambassador (row 3) is unchanged and stays disclaimed — a 180k-follower fitness and self-development audience is a consumer audience, not a purpose-led employer buying committee.
  • Follow-up (row 5), Option D (row 6) and measurement (row 7) are unchanged. AG-069's co-hosting is the mechanism that would reach the primary segment without a room, and it is blocked by D-0111 §3.

The channel that should exist and does not: peak bodies run member conferences and communities of practice, several with published calls for presentations — a gathering type reaching the primary segment directly, never researched. One researcher commission; the cheapest item on the list.

# Channel Hours a year Clients (P50) Hours per client (lower is better) Margin per hour What it is
1 Partnerships — WHS consultants, brokers, EAPs and four institutions 55 1.2 46 $209 Ten approaches to people who sit downstream of the strongest live purchase trigger in the market and lack exactly what MFC has. ⚑ Added on head-of-commercial's finding; it was missing from the first draft
2 Social — Instagram primarily, at low cadence 22 0.5 44 $217 Not a reach channel. A proof-and-capture layer for people who have already met MFC, and the reason a room encounter does not evaporate
3 The ambassador programme 15 0.2 77 $124 One monthly half-hour. Tom is first, if and when he agrees. Judgement, one door, one person — not posts. Counted honestly at nearly nothing
4 Rooms that already exist — sourcing, and standing in them 110 1.1 100 $96 Hunter walks into gatherings that already meet and teaches one Mental Rep in fifteen minutes. Two kinds of room, counted separately: 11 professional and 8 community standings, plus a recurring presence at one Inner West run club — ~485 people meet MFC
5 Follow-up, the inbox and the contact list 55 none of its own — protected by rule The unglamorous half of channels 1 and 4. Every message answered within two business days; everything into one sheet. The Instagram inbox belongs here, not to social. Never cut
6 MFC's own community — Option D, co-hosted quarterly 11 not modelled — — The option the first draft never screened and §5 now recommends. It produces registration with express consent, which no room structurally can
7 The monthly one-page, the room log and the founder questions 9 none — — The measurement that makes every row above findable-wrong. §6
TOTAL 277 3.0 ≈ 10 cohorts 92 average $104 average Against a minimum viable of 13 cohorts

Why partnerships is printed above social when social's $217 an hour is higher. ⚑ head-of-commercial ruled this, and ruled it against its own arithmetic, which is why it is worth reading rather than skipping. The gap is 4% — and partnerships' own published band (§1.1: P10 0.6 clients, P90 2.0) spans $104 to $348 an hour on the same 55 hours, an uncertainty thirty times the size of the gap. Social has no band at all, and §1.1 records that its inputs actually reproduce 1.2 clients while the plan deliberately keeps the conservative 0.5 over its own arithmetic. If either channel is ever funded further, partnerships' hours 40 to 55 are named, currently-unfunded work with a week-twelve kill criterion that fires on failure; social has no dose-response anywhere in this plan and §4.1's own evidence review concluded no. Printing social first would suggest more of the one channel MFC cannot say what more would buy.

⛔ And the honest form of the ranking is two rows, not seven. Rows 5 to 7 have no client column at all, row 3 is disclaimed, and row 4 is stated to be reach and mission rather than a lead source. The only real comparison is partnerships against social, and this plan spends two paragraphs proving it is not decidable. So, plainly: partnerships and social are indistinguishable on return per hour; ambassador and rooms are clearly lower, and rooms are kept for reach and for the mission rather than for the yield. The numbered order is a reading aid, not a finding.

⛔ This ranks what an hour already spent returns. It is not an instruction to move hours toward the top row — follow-up earns no client of its own and every client above arrives through it, community rooms are counted here at a fifth of a client and are the charity's purpose rather than a lead source, and nothing in this plan says what any channel returns if you double it. Rows 5 to 7 are 27% of the budget and are the reason rows 1 to 4 convert at all.

⚑ Two things head-of-commercial found while checking these numbers. Recorded, not absorbed.

  1. The 0.75 sourcing hour is shared, and it is charged entirely to rooms. Its own cell reads "four room asks and two partner asks." Split 4:2 it is 0.50 to rooms and 0.25 to partnerships — rooms become 99 h/yr at 82 hours per client, partnerships 66 h/yr at 55 hours per client and $174. That makes partnerships look worse and widens social's nominal lead, which is precisely why it is printed here rather than quietly adopted. The table above prints the unsplit rows so every figure traces to §1.3.
  2. ⛔ The 277 hours are free in this model and they are not free. Every margin figure in the chain is defined as after Hunter has been paid $66.10 for every costed hour — and no selling hour is costed anywhere. 277.2 × $66.10 = $18,323 of founder labour to produce ~$29,649 of margin. Net, the honest P50 year returns about $11,300. Rooms return 1.6× the cost of Hunter's own hour, ambassador 1.9×, partnerships 3.2×, social 3.3× — and a blended 1.6× is thin enough that "which channel" matters less than "whether 277 hours of unpaid selling is the right shape of year at all." That belongs in FQ11, not in a footnote. It is not a change this document is licensed to make: pricing founder selling time needs the required-personal-income figure §1.1 already records as missing.

The flex rule. ⚑ Setup trigger, added because the rule as first written locked the mechanism funding its own prerequisites: in the first eight weeks the ceiling lifts on the setup work itself (§1.3), with no proposal required. After that, up to eight hours in a week where a written proposal has been issued to a named organisation — capped at six weeks a year, tracked on the one-page, and the extra comes from social, then ambassador, then sourcing, never from follow-up. Down to 0.75 hours — follow-up only — in any week where two Foundations cohorts are contracted and undelivered. Delivery beats pipeline.

The three sentences that make it work

  1. MFC does not talk about mental fitness in these rooms. It teaches one rep and the room does one. Fifteen minutes, from the workshop's own P3a–P7, so nothing is invented and nothing is freelanced.
  2. The room is not the buyer, and the best rooms are not rooms at all. A run club produces people; a chamber breakfast produces a few doors; a WHS consultant with a client under an improvement notice produces a deadline. All three are in the plan, in separate columns, at their honest rates.
  3. Nothing is captured by MFC. People capture themselves. A person photographs a handle and sends one word from their own phone. It works with no website, no form, no privacy determination and no wifi.

The two dependencies, named once

  • The live website does not sell, describe or mention any of the four products, and /radar is a 404 (verified 2026-09-04). This plan is built to work without it. ⚑ But the website is built by Alysse Curran, not by Hunter — so ~2.5 hours of her time, costing this plan nothing, is listed separately at §6.3 rather than treated as competing with sourcing rooms. That confusion was a real error in the first draft, found by head-of-digital.
  • MFC has no privacy determination. So nothing here builds a list MFC collects. The capture mechanism in §2.3 is a person sending MFC a message, which is the one form of contact that carries its own consent.

These are named here and not again.

PART TWO — THE WORKING

1 · THE ARITHMETIC (G)

1.1 · G1 — the chain, from four client organisations backwards

Where thirteen cohorts comes from. 15-THE-MONEY-CASE: thirteen Foundations engagements a year returns $38,257 above every cost including paying Hunter — the minimum-viable tier. Goal is 34 and is not reachable by one person in five hours a week; nobody should pretend otherwise.

⚠ And "thirteen" is a formula output, not a known target. The money case §6 states plainly that all three inputs a minimum-income figure actually requires are missing from the vault — Hunter's available hours, his required personal income, and MFC's fixed annual costs — and that MFC's GST position is contradicted inside its own documents, moving every figure by up to 10%. This plan inherits that limit. Stated once, here.

The unit of sale is a client organisation, not a booking. A 120-person employer is six cohorts on one relationship. Every rate below converts to a client, not to a transaction.

Every conversion rate is an ASSUMPTION, marked once and then committed to. MFC has zero delivered cohorts, zero sales, and has never quoted a price or issued a proposal.

Ref Assumption P10 P50 P90 Basis
X1 Room attendee → messages MFC 15% 30% 45% ASSUMPTION. ⚑ Set separately for community rooms at 15% (P50) on head-of-community's finding — an Instagram-DM capture in a Men's Shed is not a 30% mechanism
X2 Contact → can open an organisational door — tiered, below — ⚑ A single X2 was the first draft's worst number. It was carried entirely by four entries now moved to channel 2
X3 Door → scoped conversation 40% 60% 75% ASSUMPTION

X4p Partner-referred conversation → client 15% ⚑ 20% — the same as X4 32% ⚑ Was 32%, then briefly 24%, and 24% did not reproduce: 6 scoped × 0.24 = 1.44, while the plan printed 1.2. Set to X4 on the adversarial pass's own recommendation — "an organisation that has never issued a proposal has no basis for claiming partner-referred leads close better than its own." 6 × 0.20 = 1.2 exactly, and the printed number is now the computed one
X6 Professional room size — 25 — ASSUMPTION, replaced on booking each slot. ⚠ head-of-community flagged that the same question it asked of X7 is worth asking here: seven of the sixteen professional rooms are Rotary evening dinner meetings, which in Australia skew substantially retiree and semi-retiree. Not answered in this pass
X7 Community room size — ⚑ 20 — and it was 30 — ⛔ 30 was inherited from a room list that no longer exists. A Probus general meeting plausibly seats 30; a P&C business meeting and a Men's Shed session do not. ⚑ And for the strongest room the number is not an assumption at all: Fort Street High School P&C publishes attendance by name in its 2026 minutes — 22, 18, 19, 34, mean ≈ 23 — and three of those four were held wholly or partly online, which the standing-up fifteen minutes does not fit. Weighted across the eight standings (1 P&C at 23 sourced, 1 P&C at 15, 2 Probus at 30, 3 shed sessions at 15, 1 facilitated group at 12) ≈ 19, carried as 20. The seven non-Fort-Street sizes remain ASSUMPTIONS

Channel 2 — partnerships → ⚑ RE-MODELLED to 1.0–1.5 clients (P50 1.2), down from 2.0 (§7.1).

Ten approaches → ~4 partner conversations → ~3 partners who engage → ~2 qualified referrals each = 6 scoped conversations. ⛔ Five compounded unsourced rates, reported as a P50 with no band at all — the banding discipline this plan is proud of was applied to X1/X3/X4 and skipped on the channel producing the most clients. And X4p = 32% was the highest rate in the model, sitting on the least-tested channel.

Clients
P10 — two-thirds on each rate, X4p = X4 = 20% 0.6
P50 — ⚑ X4p set to 20%, i.e. X4 1.2
P90 — the original assumption 2.0

Why X4p drops to X4. An organisation that has never issued a proposal has no basis for claiming partner-referred leads close better than its own — and the intermediate 24% had the additional problem that it did not reproduce the number printed beside it. And a WHS consultant referring their own client under an improvement notice will diligence MFC harder than a chamber secretary will — finding no product page, no price ever quoted, no delivered cohort, and no certificate of currency (§3.3).

⛔ The channel-conflict objection, which the first draft never named: a WHS consultant with a client under an improvement notice has every commercial incentive to deliver the training themselves and bill for it. The plan treated them purely as distribution. The answer is the one R1 already gives and this plan should say out loud: MFC is not selling training, it is selling the delivery-and-measurement artefact the consultant cannot produce and their client now needs. That is also why the trade is non-competing — the consultant keeps the client relationship and the risk assessment; MFC supplies the skill layer and the evidence of control effectiveness. If a partner can do both, they are not a partner and the criterion at §1.4 will say so by week twelve.

⚠ R1 is dated 2026-08-10 and its market claims were not re-verified for this plan. Re-check before the four-hour build.

Channel 1b — community rooms → ⚑ 0.1 clients, and ~210 people.

⛔ Recomputed after the run-club correction. The plan applied a thirty-person room size to a weekly run club while saying itself that it "cannot be a stage" — and being weekly, it carried the largest share of the twenty community standings. Removing it takes twelve standings of thirty out of the count. ⚑ Which club this is became a live question on 2026-09-05 and §2.2 does not close it: the 440 at Bronte Beach is ~40 minutes each way from the Inner West and is a relationship Hunter already has; Woodstock Runners is fifteen minutes away and cold, in a room type he has said he is not in. The ~50 is carried unchanged either way and is replaced by the room log's own count (§6.1 Tab 2) rather than defended — ⚠ and the skeptic pass is right that a membership club plausibly yields ~15 distinct people over twelve mornings where a high-turnover social club yields ~50. That is untested in both directions.**

8 community standings × X7 (20) = 160 encounters, plus the run club as a recurring presence — roughly twelve mornings, ~4 real conversations each = ~50 — for ~210 community encounters.

⛔ And a flat X2 of 3% across this list was wrong in shape, not only in level (head-of-community, 2026-09-05). It implied that a Men's Shed, a Probus club, the Older Women's Network and a school P&C differ only in size. They differ in kind, and two of them differ in medium: §2.3's entire capture mechanism is an Instagram DM, and in four sheds, two Probus clubs and OWN — median age sixty-five plus — that is not a channel with a lower rate, it is a channel that is absent.

Three things this does not do, marked rather than smoothed.

  1. It does not re-rate X2 for the professional rooms, and it probably should. Tier B's 10% was set against an undefined buyer. Against a primary segment of 20–200-staff purpose-led employers, a chamber breakfast and a Rotary dinner are aimed at a segment ranked fourth. I have not invented a lower rate, because there is no basis for one — and X6 above already shows the question was open. The correct fix is not arithmetic, it is observation: the first two room visits answer it. One line is added to the room log at §6.1 for exactly this.
  2. It does not re-model the hours freed by moving the community rooms out. ⚠ (The ~17.5-hour figure is 10 rooms × §1.3's 1.75 h per on-site date, and this plan funds 8 community standings, not 10 — so the real figure is nearer 14 h. Corrected 2026-09-05; the point is unaffected and the number was wrong.) Those are the most contested hours in this plan. Whether they go to partnerships (where the segment is), to the mission line, or back to Hunter is a founder question — 04-THE-STRATEGY.md FQ5 and FQ1 together — not an arithmetic result.
  3. It inherits this plan's own disclosed limit and does not fix it. "Thirteen is a formula output, not a known target" — all three inputs a minimum-income figure requires are missing, and MFC's GST position is contradicted inside its own documents, moving every figure by up to 10%.

And the honest headline, corrected: the segment decision changes no client number at all. The plan was ~3 clients against a minimum viable of 13 before and it is ~3 after. What changed is where the hours go and what the plan is honest about — one channel is now demonstrably aimed at a named buyer, one is marked unverified rather than counted, one has stopped pretending to be a funnel, and four rooms that served nobody are gone. A more honest plan at exactly the same number. The shortfall is untouched, and pretending a re-sort dented it would have been the easiest lie in this document.

⚑ The retiree rooms need a second capture, and it costs nothing (§2.3). In a Men's Shed, a Probus club or OWN, "message me the word ATTEMPT" reaches almost nobody. Ask the organiser to put the THE REP link in the next newsletter or on the notice board. MFC transcribes nothing, holds nothing and needs no privacy determination — AG-016 is satisfied by construction — and it is the one thing organisers are always willing to do.

Read those next to each other. Eight community standings and a weekly presence produce ~210 people and a tenth of a client. Eleven professional standings produce 278 people and a whole one. Ten partnership approaches produce no people at all and two. The difference is not effort — it is who is standing there. All three stay: ~210 people meeting mental fitness is the charity's purpose and costs the same hour. The plan never again reports a community room as a lead source, and the eight retiree rooms are now counted at zero doors by rule rather than at a low rate.

Channel 4 — social and inbound → 0.5 clients. ⚑ Corrected on head-of-commercial's H1 check: the first version's inputs did not reproduce its own output (4 a month × 12 × ⅛ × 20% = 1.2 clients, not 0.5). The conservative headline is kept and the inputs are fixed, not the other way round — taking the other branch would inflate the total to 4.7 clients on an entirely untested channel, which is exactly the P80-dressed-as-a-P50 failure the banding exists to remove. ~1.7 inbound messages a month (≈20 a year), 1 in 8 becoming a scoped conversation (ASSUMPTION) → 2.5 scoped conversations → ~0.5 clients at X4.

Channel 5 — ambassador → 0.2 clients. ⚑ With its inputs, which it did not have.

~12 posts a year Tom chooses to make (the floor asks for none, so this is a guess at what a willing person does) × ~1 inbound message naming a rep per post = ~12 inbound → 1 in 8 becomes a scoped conversation (the same rate as social, ASSUMPTION) = 1.5 scoped × X4 (20%) = 0.3. ⛔ Discounted to 0.2 for the one structural reason the plan states elsewhere: §3.3 imposes no call to action to MFC for the first six months, so half the year cannot produce an attributable inbound at all. The floor's own asks — one door and one person brought a year — are counted in §3.2 and are not clients.

This is the smallest number in the plan and it is now derived rather than asserted.

Total, P50 as originally modelled: 1.0 + 2.0 + 0.3 + 0.5 + 0.2 = 4.0 clients ≈ 13 cohorts.

⛔ Honest total after partnerships was re-modelled (1.2), the run-club correction propagated, and community re-modelled at 0.1 on head-of-community's 2026-09-05 findings: 1.0 + 1.2 + 0.1 + 0.5 + 0.2 = 3.0 clients ≈ 10 cohorts — against a minimum viable of 13.

⛔ With partnerships re-modelled at 1.2, the run club no longer counted at room size, and community at 0.1, the honest P50 is ~3.0 clients ≈ 10 cohorts — against a minimum viable of 13.

⛔ And the 3.0 is itself unheld, which the document has to say rather than imply. 1.0 of it — the whole professional room channel — rests on X1 = 30%, and §2.2 states that this rate "should be treated as unheld until at least two of the first four asks come back," because twenty-six of the thirty rooms publish no evidence that they want an outside speaker. At X1's own P10 of 15% the professional channel halves to 0.5 and the total is ~2.5 clients ≈ 8 cohorts.

⛔ A second, larger sensitivity sits underneath it and this pass did not resolve it. Five of the eight VERIFIED professional rooms are Rotary evening dinner meetings, which X6's own cell concedes "skew substantially retiree and semi-retiree" and which rows 7 and 11 concede publish no speaker offer of their own. Apply this plan's own tier-E logic to those five and professional clients fall from 1.0 to ~0.55 — the total goes to ~2.55, about eight cohorts. Every premise for that calculation is already in this document; only the arithmetic was missing. It is named here rather than absorbed, because deciding it needs the first four asks, not another pass.

⛔ There is also no ask → yes rate anywhere in this model. The funnel is banded at X1, X3, X4, X4p, X6 and X7 and starts at "encounter" — nineteen standings a year are an input, not an output. §2.2's own numbers imply roughly fifteen yeses against ~176 room asks a year, an ~8.5% ask→yes rate that is nowhere written down and therefore cannot be banded, killed or found wrong. It needs to exist as X0 with no number, so that its absence is visible. ⚑ The community correction moves the total by a tenth of a client and does not move the cohort headline — which is the point: the community rooms were never the load-bearing number, and now the document says so with an arithmetic that shows it. That is the number in the headline at the top of this document, and it is the one Hunter should plan against. The 4.0 is retained above so the change is visible rather than silently substituted.

1.2 · The band, because one number cannot be found wrong honestly

Clients Cohorts Revenue above cost What it means
P10 2 ~6 ⚑ ~$14,900 One number, computed with fixed costs held constant. 6 × $2,942.82 = $17,657 of loaded margin — but the ×1.4 recovers overhead in proportion to delivery volume, so at six engagements it returns ~$3,270 against ~$6,000 of assumed fixed costs, a $2,730 shortfall that must come out of the margin. $17,657 − $2,730 = ~$14,900. Below minimum viable, and low volume does not merely earn less — it fails to pay MFC's fixed costs at all

1.4 · G3 — kill criteria and review dates

⚑ Four of the first draft's six criteria were rewritten — head-of-commercial showed that three could never fire and one would fire on a plan that was exactly on track. A waived kill criterion is worse than none, because it teaches the others to be ignored.

Channel Kill criterion Review date
Professional rooms ⚑ DATE-GATED, not volume-gated (§7.1). By 31 March 2027: fewer than 6 doors, or fewer than 2 scoped conversations. (Gating on "after 8 standings" made it unevaluable — at ~11 standings a year, standing 8 lands around month nine.) Original wording: after 8 standings: fewer than 6 doors, or fewer than 2 scoped conversations. (Was "fewer than 3 doors" — expected doors from 8 rooms is ~8 at the tiered rate, so 3 separated catastrophe from disaster rather than on-plan from off-plan.) Either triggers a change of room type, not more rooms After standing 8, or 31 Dec 2026
Partnerships ⚑ REWRITTEN (§7.1). Ten approaches made and fewer than two partner conversations by week twelve. (The previous test — "three or more partners engage warmly and none refers" — was gated on success: if only one partner engaged it could never fire, and the channel carrying the most clients would persist forever. That is the identical defect §1.4 claims to have removed from three others, reintroduced in the biggest channel.) An activity-gated test fires on failure rather than on success Week 12
⚑ Option D — the co-hosted quarterly session Two sessions run and no co-host willing to bring their list for a third. (Option D exists precisely because it borrows someone else's audience; a co-hosted session MFC ends up promoting alone is Option A wearing a different name, at Option A's cost.) Recorded against channel = co-host (§6.1). ⛔ Added on the independent review's G3 finding (§7.4): A, B and C each had a kill criterion and the one Hunter is actually asked to accept did not After session 2
Community rooms ⛔ Never killed on leads. Killed only if average attendance is below 10 across five standings — i.e. the reach is not real either. ⚑ Was below 15, and head-of-community showed that broke when X7 moved from 30 to 20: Fort Street P&C's own published attendance runs 18–34 and a Men's Shed session is ~15, so a threshold of 15 would have fired on rooms performing exactly as modelled. A criterion that fires on on-plan performance is the defect §1.4 exists to have removed, reintroduced by a change made elsewhere 31 Dec 2026
Follow-up Median time from a person's message to a human reply exceeds five business days, twice in a quarter. Response is to cut rooms, not to try harder Monthly
Social Twelve weeks at the recommended cadence with zero inbound messages naming a room, a rep or a post. Not "low engagement" — zero inbound 12 weeks from first post
Ambassador No monthly session held for three consecutive months, or the ambassador says at the two-question check that it has stopped being fun. The second is the one that matters Month 3, then quarterly
⚑ Mental Fitness Month (November) Not a channel, deliberately. 44-the-24-month-strategy.md defers MFM to 2028: nine of ten comparable Australian norm campaigns were funded by government, a statutory authority, a levy or an industry consortium, and none by a small charity unaided. The Theory of Change fails the 2026 event on RECORD. A mass challenge is a reach instrument aimed at a maintenance problem, and MFC has five hours a week. Ruled in one line so the omission is a decision, not an oversight. What MFC does take at zero cost: cluster the §2.2 outreach so rooms land in November, when chambers, Rotary, AHRI and peak bodies are already trying to fill wellbeing slots. A sequencing edit, not a campaign n/a
The whole plan ⚑ AND BECOMES OR (§7.1). At the review: fewer than 8 scoped conversations, OR zero proposals accepted. ⛔ The AND version was defeatable by the single easiest action in the plan — issuing one proposal, which requires nobody's agreement but Hunter's, would have bought another six months regardless of how few conversations happened. A founder could pass his own review by writing one document. (And "issued" becomes "accepted": issuing is an activity, accepting is a result.) ⚑ 30 Apr 2027, not 5 March — the March date judged a plan whose months 3–5 fell in the Australian summer dead zone, giving it ~15 usable weeks. 5 Sep 2027 (revenue)

1.5 · ⛔ THE MISSING HALF — what happens after "scoped conversation"

The single largest structural gap in this plan, and it was invisible because the document is thorough everywhere else (§7.1). The funnel is specified in obsessive detail from stranger → encounter → contact → conversation — and then stops dead at the exact step MFC has never performed.

There is no price script, no scoping-call structure, no proposal template, no contract, and no answer to "how much?" — the single most predictable question in the whole plan. §2.4a's positioning sentence ends "it's what I'd talk to you about if you ever wanted it" and there was no next line anywhere in the document. The plan carries four scripted answers for clinical questions and none for a pricing question.

X4 = 20% — the rate that produces every dollar — was a number attached to nothing.

And there is no learning curve. X4 is applied from month one to a founder who has never issued a proposal or quoted a price. The realistic conversion on the first three scoping calls is zero, and the six-month test would then fire on a man who is still learning to sell.

The four artefacts, and they are month-one work

# Artefact What it must contain Cost
1 The price answer, spoken The sentence Hunter says when an organiser or a partner asks "how much?" — in a room, unprepared. It must name a band, not dodge: prices are ratified (D-0113, D-0125) and dodging a ratified price reads as either shame or improvisation. This is the most-needed sentence in the plan and it does not exist. 0.5h
2 The scoping call, structured 30 minutes: what the group is, how many, what is going on for them, what they have tried, who decides, when, what it costs, what happens next. Ends with a named next step and a date, or it was not a scoped conversation — which is also the plan's own definition of the funnel step 1h
3 The proposal One page. What is delivered, over what period, for how many people, at what price, what is measured, what report comes back, and what MFC does not claim (P18, P19). Reusable, so it is written once 2h
4 The contract and cancellation terms D-0122 already governs cancellation and validity. This is assembly, not authorship 1h

Total ~4.5 hours, once, in month one — and it is in the ~10.9-hour month-one figure at §1.3.

⚠ This section names the gap and specifies the artefacts. It does not write them — that is head-of-commercial and head-of-delivery work against 07-BUYER-PACK.md and D-0113/D-0122, and doing it inside a lead-generation plan would be the fourth wording problem all over again. What matters is that it is now a named, costed, month-one dependency rather than an invisible hole between two boxes.

1.6 · ⛔ WHAT MFC DOES IF IT IS NOT WORKING — the P10 branch

§1.2 states the downside case and the first draft had no plan for it. The plan planned for the good failure (P90: a second facilitator) and not the bad one, in an organisation with ~$12,000.

⚑ P10 is ~$14,900, and §1.2 now prints that one number rather than the linear $17,657. The correction: the ×1.4 recovers overhead in proportion to delivery volume, so at six engagements it returns ~$3,270 against ~$6,000 of assumed fixed costs, and the $2,730 shortfall comes out of the margin. An earlier version printed the linear figure and then declared it wrong a page later, which left two numbers in the document and no way to tell which one Hunter should plan against.

Trigger Date What happens
Fewer than 4 scoped conversations 31 Jan 2027 (four months in, before the review) Stop sourcing new rooms. The problem is not volume — it is the proposition or the room type. Spend the sourcing hour on the five existing warmest contacts instead
Fewer than 8 scoped conversations, or zero proposals accepted 30 Apr 2027 The whole-plan criterion (§1.4). Lead generation is not the answer to what is wrong; stop and re-plan
Cash below $6,000 Whenever it happens ⛔ A condition-5 question and it goes straight to Hunter and the committee. This plan does not have a view on what MFC does about the founder's income and must not pretend to

This is stated rather than solved, deliberately. What MFC does at P10 involves Hunter's own income and MFC's survival — conditions 5 and 6, and the committee's, not this document's.

1.7 · ⚑ The founder's own exposure — the safeguard built for the volunteer and not for him

§3.3 builds a control for Tom: "send it, close the app, and message Hunter — not because Hunter can fix it, but because Tom should not be alone with it." That is exactly right.

⛔ Nobody is that person for Hunter — and this plan puts him in ~31 rooms a year where, by design (§2.5, AG-015), he stays fifteen minutes afterwards precisely so that a stranger can disclose to him. He is given a script, told to write nothing down, and told to stop. There was no debrief, no cap, and no one he is required to tell.

He built MFC after losing two men in construction. The plan asks him to stand in rooms of men like them, thirty-one times a year, and absorb whatever comes.
After any room where someone disclosed to him He tells one named person — a committee member, and Eimear Quigley is the obvious offer to make — the same week. Not for advice. The same function §3.3 gives Tom
A cap No more than five rooms in any calendar month, whatever the sourcing hour produces
Cost Nothing

2 · INTO EXISTING COMMUNITIES (E) — the channel Hunter named, and the one that reaches people

2.1 · E1 — the fifteen minutes, minute by minute

This is not a talk about MFC. It is one rep, taught, and done by the room. Every line is drawn from 02-MERGED-DESIGN-ALL-THREE-PRODUCTS.md P3a–P7 — the same words as the paid product, compressed. Nothing here is new material and no claim here is off 11-PERMITTED-CLAIMS.md.

⚑ The single biggest change the heads made to this plan. The first draft's room watched Hunter run a rep on his own desk scenario and then wrote an intention. head-of-product's finding: planning is not attempting, so the sheet's own most-protected sentence — "a rep counts when you attempt it" — was not yet true of anybody in the room. The 12:00 block is now a covert self-run rep, at zero minute cost. Watching someone else's rep produces "that was good." Attempting your own produces "I did something" — and only one of those survives nine hours and gets typed into a DM.

Kit: one printed A5 card, and his own phone. ⚑ The card is new, on head-of-product's finding. A phone held up is unreadable past about two metres, in glare, or at the back of a 200-person run club — the least accessible artefact in the plan. Front: the handle and the word, in very large type. Back: the P3a definition, one sentence. It is not a leave-behind — they photograph it, nobody takes it — it costs a few dollars, and it gives a room with elevated hearing loss (a Men's Shed, a construction site) the one sentence they must be able to repeat. The delivery is otherwise 100% auditory with zero redundancy, and two of the four community rooms are exactly the wrong population for that.

Minutes Part What he does Source
0:00–0:45 Open. ⚑ The hook comes first, the disclaimers second. "Quick question, and don't answer out loud. Think of the last time you didn't handle a moment the way you'd have liked to. Not a crisis — an ordinary one. I'd guess it wasn't that long ago.

I'm Hunter, I run a charity called Mental Fitness Collective. In the next fifteen minutes I'm going to teach you one thing you can use on that moment. Not talk about it — teach it, and you're going to do it once, in here.

I'm not a psychologist. This is education and skills practice, not therapy or crisis support. You don't have to speak, share anything, or explain why you're passing."		 P1, P31. Reordered (§7.1 M2) — the first draft opened on three disclaimers. ⚑ "Most people don't have to think back very far" was cut (§7.1 C4): a population claim, and §2.5's own never-say list bars exactly that shape.
0:45–2:15 P3a — what mental fitness actually is. ⛔ NEVER CUT, at any length. "We all have a body. Physical health is how it's doing. Physical fitness is what it can actually do... We define it this way: mental health is how you're doing. Mental fitness is what you can actually do — how well you think, how you work with what you feel, how you act on what matters, and how you connect." Then: "It's a capacity, not perfection. You will miss moments. I still do. That isn't this failing — that's what a capacity is." P3a verbatim; P2, P4
2:15–4:30 P3 — the honest limit "Can fifteen minutes do anything? Not much on its own." Two examples — driving and the bins. Someone correcting you, a schedule you didn't set, a consequence you could see. "Nothing in your day corrects you on this. Nobody marks it. When you miss one of these moments the day just carries on. That's not a willpower failure, that's a missing feedback loop." Close on: "It's not you trying harder. It's what's around you." P3 compressed; P34
4:30–5:30 P4 — the four On his fingers. "Right now four things are going on. You're thinking. You're feeling — bored is a feeling. You're acting — sitting still is an action. And you're connecting, or not." Then: ⚑ "You can't always change what you're feeling. Changing what you're doing is often the easier door." P4. ⚑ "You can nearly always change what you're doing" was rewritten (§7.1 C4) — a near-universal agency claim, and K-08 row 5 bars "anyone can shift any moment if they try hard enough"
5:30–8:00 P5 — Stop. ⚑ Scenario swaps by room type. Professional rooms: scenario one as written — "Twenty to four on a Wednesday. A message lands. The words are fine. The tone isn't. You read it twice." Community rooms: P8's scenario three — the twelve steps from the car to the front door, "where's my library bag" — or, for the trade network and the shed, P8's scenario two, the handover. (Both already written verbatim in the source. Nothing new is authored.) Then the pause, then he answers it himself, both sides. Then: "Stop is noticing a moment is happening while it's still happening. That's all." P5 + P8. ⚑ On head-of-product's finding (§7.6): the first draft ran a desk scenario in a 5am beach run club and a Men's Shed. The parent design flags this exact failure at P8 — "that first one was a desk and a lot of people don't have one" — and P8 is the part the compression drops. So the compression removed the repair and kept the thing needing repair
⚑ The pause, by room Seated indoor rooms: the full three seconds. Outdoor and noisy rooms (an early-morning outdoor run club, a shed with a compressor, a breakfast with cutlery): one beat, and he answers straight into it. Three seconds of nothing in a noisy room reads as a stumble, not as a mechanism The both-sides mechanic survives; the silence does not have to
8:00–10:15 P6 — Shift "Shift is changing one thing so the moment becomes workable. Not calm. Nobody has ever calmed down because someone told them to. If your fix needs a quiet room and ten minutes it will never happen at twenty to four on a Wednesday." Two doors — state or thinking. The test: "can I do the next thing from here?" And the State Check: "is there too much in me right now, too little, or is this workable?" — "you're not scoring it and I'm never going to ask you for your answer." P6
10:15–12:00 P7 — Do, and the unit The options — send it in an hour, walk over, send the boring version, or send exactly what you were going to send having actually decided to. Then, verbatim and in no other wording: "A rep counts when you attempt it, aimed at something that matters, whether or not it works." And the stall image: "Same as a learner who stalls at the lights. That lesson still counted. It probably counted more." P7; P10 buyer form (c), verbatim. ⛔ §7.1 C2: the first draft used "A rep counts when you attempt it. Not when it works" in three places. That is a fourth wording, which P10 forbids, and it is the exact inversion the permitted-claims page records catching and fixing on 2 September — it says a rep does not count when it works. Corrected in all three
12:00–13:30 ⚑ THE ROOM DOES A REP. ⛔ NEVER CUT. "Ninety seconds. In your head or on your phone. Nobody sees it, I'm not going to ask, and there's nowhere for it to go.

First — pick the moment. If nothing comes, take one of these three: the message you're going to reread twice. The first ninety seconds after you walk in the door. The conversation you already know is coming this week. [3 seconds]

Now run it, on that moment, with me. Stop — what's the first sign you'd get that it's happening? … Shift — what's the one small thing available to you right there? Not calm. Available. … Do — what's the next move, toward what actually matters to you in that bit of your life? …

That's a rep. You just did one." ⚑ A two-sentence gloss closed this block in an earlier version — "it doesn't count because it was good, it counts because you attempted it" — and head-of-compliance cut it (§7.2). It is not the banned inversion and it is not wrong, but it is another wording of P10, ninety seconds after the plan tells Hunter to say P10(c) verbatim and in no other wording, in the one document delivered aloud with no leave-behind. The verbatim sentence has already been said at 10:15.**		 P10 compressed; P13, P10(c). ⚑ Two findings applied: the covert self-run rep (§7.6 P1), and the three prompts (§7.6 P3) — AG-147, Debra, HARD: "you've got to give people prompts, 100%." The workshop solves blank-page moment selection with panels 5 and 6 of the leave-behind; the fifteen-minute version has no leave-behind and still said "pick a moment"
13:30–15:00 Close, the limit, the crisis line, and the ask The limit: "A skill doesn't replace a bad environment. If the conditions are genuinely against you, this is not the answer to that." ⚑ Then, new and never cut: "And if any of this is live for you right now — Lifeline, 13 11 14, any time." Then: "We're a charity. We've never delivered a measured cohort — we've got no results to show you and we're not going to invent any." ⚑ Then the one line that travels: "If you tell one person one thing from today, tell them this — mental health is how you're doing, mental fitness is what you can actually do." Then the ask (§2.3). P14, P19, P2. ⚑ The crisis line (§7.1 C4): twenty-five unscreened strangers heard about difficult moments and only those who messaged got a crisis number. ⚑ The tell-one-person line (§7.1 MV3): ~485 people were leaving holding a technique with the category riding along as context

⛔ He stays fifteen minutes after every room. AG-015, Debra's verbal duty-of-care position, is applied to the paid workshop and was absent from the fifteen minutes — which has less structure, not more. And there is a fourth prepared answer, for "that's me", at §2.5.

The cut order — 15 → 10 → 5 minutes

⚑ Recomputed. The first draft's order had an arithmetic error of exactly the class the source document records catching four times — a cut costed against a part an earlier cut had already reduced. It cut P3 by 1.0 and then claimed a further 1.5 from a part that was by then 1.25 minutes long, so its "ten-minute version" actually landed at about 10:30.

Cut Saves Leaves
1. P4's four → named in one sentence, not enumerated −0.75 14:15
2. P3's second example (the bins) — driving alone carries it −1.0 13:15
3. P6's State Check −0.75 12:30
4. The professional-room positioning sentence (§2.4a) — ⚑ it is NOT a block in the table above −0.75 only in a professional room, where the sentence is actually spoken and the delivery therefore runs 15:45, not 15:00 11:45
5. P3 down to its two load-bearing sentences: nothing corrects you / it's not you trying harder −1.0 10:45
6. The self-run rep from 90 → 45 seconds, narrated faster — ⛔ not deleted −0.75 10:00
7. P5's second reading of the scenario — held in reserve, not needed for ten −0.25 9:45

⚑ A fourth correction, from the independent review (§7.4): cut 4 credited 45 seconds that are not in the fifteen-minute table. The table sums to exactly 15.00 without the §2.4a positioning sentence — so a professional-room delivery actually runs 15:45, and the ladder's saving from cut 4 is real only there. In a community room, where the sentence is never spoken, the ten-minute version needs one more cut and cut 7 stops being a reserve. The table below is correct as an arithmetic ladder; what was wrong was the claim that the same ladder describes both room types.

⚑ Recomputed a second time on head-of-product's H1 check (§7.2). The first recomputation fixed the compounding error and then introduced a fresh ten-second slip at row 1 that propagated through all seven rows — 15:00 − 0:45 is 14:15, not 14:25 — so the "ten-minute version" was really 9:45. This is the third time this table has been wrong, and it is the part a facilitator reads while improvising under time pressure. Corrected above, and it is now better than merely accurate: cut 6 lands the ten-minute version at exactly 10:00, which makes cut 7 a genuine reserve rather than a needed cut — and keeping a second reading of the scenario is worth more in a noisy outdoor room than ten seconds of margin.

⛔ Never cut, at any length — four things, and the first draft protected only two of them: P3a · the unit in its P10(c) wording · ⚑ the self-run rep (the source's own never-cut list includes P10 on the grounds that it is "the only segment that produces something that survives the room" — the first draft cut it at step 4) · ⚑ the eight-second not-a-psychologist line (it lived entirely in the open, and the first draft's five-minute version deleted the open — so the shortest, most improvised delivery was the one where the claims disclaimer silently vanished).

The five-minute version: 8s disclaimer + P3a (1:30) + one scenario run straight through Stop-Shift-Do (1:45) + the unit (0:30) + the self-run rep (0:30) + the limit, crisis line and ask (0:40) = 5:03. ⚑ The first version of this build summed to 5:18 (§7.2). The fifteen seconds come out of the scenario run and out of nothing else — not P3a and not the self-run rep, both of which are on the never-cut list, and shaving the rep is the exact failure the covert-rep change was made to prevent. At five minutes the ask is the photo only, no codeword.

2.2 · E2 — the named gatherings, in the Inner West

Thirty rooms. Every one was found by fetching a real page on 2026-09-05; none is named from general knowledge. Nineteen are VERIFIED against the strict grade below, against E2's floor of fifteen.

Two researcher passes ran — one on business and professional rooms, one on community and service rooms — across Marrickville, Newtown, Leichhardt, Balmain, Annandale, Dulwich Hill, Petersham, Ashfield, Summer Hill, Stanmore, Enmore, Rozelle, Glebe, Camperdown, Sydenham, Erskineville, Alexandria, the immediately adjacent suburbs where an Inner West club actually meets (Abbotsford, Concord, Drummoyne, Five Dock, Burwood, Haberfield, Lilyfield), and the CBD where it is a short train trip. Why the section had to be rebuilt at all is below the count table, not in front of the rooms.

⚑ J2 — the thirty rooms, re-sorted against the primary segment, 2026-09-05

These rooms were assembled before MFC had a segment definition. Sorted against [[60-projects/lead-generation-v3/04-THE-STRATEGY|04-THE-STRATEGY.md]]'s recommended primary — purpose-led small-to-medium employers, 20–200 staff, Sydney — sixteen could reach a decision-maker in a named segment, ten serve the mission and reach no buyer, and four serve neither.

⛔ And the number that matters most: rooms reaching the recommended primary segment directly — zero. The plan's segment-reaching capacity does not live in this list. It lives in channel 2, where the four peak-body entries were moved "AHRI, CCWT/ACWA, NDS, LGNSW/StateCover → channel 2, partnerships. Not rooms."

⚑ The ruling on the ten mission rooms: they leave this plan and get their own line. This plan already concedes the substance — "the plan never again reports a community room as a lead source" — and a line item structurally incapable of producing this plan's output unit is not an underperforming channel; it is a different programme sharing a budget. Three reasons, none presentational:

  1. It corrupts the arithmetic reader. "Eight community standings and a weekly presence produce ~210 people and a tenth of a client" does two incompatible jobs in one table. A reader optimising the lead plan cuts these rooms; a reader optimising the mission defends them. The merged table lets neither win cleanly.
  2. The hours are the binding constraint. Ten rooms at §1.3's 1.75 h per on-site date is ~17.5 hours a year against a five-hour week. Inside a lead-gen plan those hours are unjustifiable and will lose every prioritisation argument. Inside a mission line they are defensible on their own terms.
  3. They need different metrics, and 26-social-architecture-and-chapter-model.md §4.2 already specifies them — sites opened, site survival at 3/6/12 months, attendance and returning-attendee rate, incidents logged, moments named and still in use at 3 months — and explicitly not rep counts or wellbeing scores. None of those belong in a funnel.

The mission line's justification is not thin. AG-047 (Eimear, 24 Aug): "it doesn't matter that you start small... Because you build from it." AG-152 (Debra): the purpose is "a community of leaders that are trying to implement it" — not just a lead list. And these rooms hold the only recurring practice rhythm MFC has actually found — this section's own inversion, below: "a repeat presence is available in exactly the rooms that cannot give a slot." Every room with a genuine recurring rhythm is in group (b).

What separating them costs, stated openly: this plan loses ~0.1 clients and its "~210 people" reach line. It should. And the mission line must then survive its own funding argument on hours, which it may not, at five hours a week. That is the real decision, and separating the lines makes it visible instead of hidden. It is a founder question and it is recorded in 04-THE-STRATEGY.md as FQ5.

The room rows themselves are unchanged below. Nothing has been deleted from the record; the four removed rooms stay documented so they are not re-proposed.

⛔ Why this section was rebuilt from nothing

The previous version was an Eastern Suburbs room list — Bronte nine times, Randwick four, Clovelly twice, Bondi once — for a founder who works in the Inner West. Hunter, 2026-09-05: "i am also located in the inner west - not eastern suburbs." The vault records nothing about where he lives — and, as later checking showed, it did record two of the rooms he is already in, in a transcript neither build searched. The first builder filled the gap with plausible invention — and the independent reviewer's note that the six newest rooms were "named from general knowledge of Sydney, not from a directory" was published as a stated gap rather than treated as a defect. A note that a factual claim is unverified is not a disclosure; it is a defect with a label on it.

What the search found that invention would have got wrong — five real findings

Each of these is a fact the previous version would have fabricated in the opposite direction.

  1. ⛔ There is no "Inner West Business Chamber." It does not exist under that name. Inner West Council's own directory lists seven chambers and associations and no umbrella body. "IWC" in the local event listings is Inner West Council, not a chamber — a plan that guessed would have written to an organisation that is not there.
  2. ⛔ The Rotary Club of Balmain could not be verified to exist. It appears in neither District 9675's nor District 9685's full club directory (both fetched today; 53 clubs in the first), and its only trace, rotarynews.info/club4401, returns 404 on both http and https. The meeting details circulating in search results come from a cached copy of a page that no longer resolves. This does not prove the club has folded, but it is the profile of one that has. It is deleted from this plan and is not on any call list.
  3. ⚠ No Leichhardt Chamber of Commerce was found, and that is a weaker claim than the one an earlier draft made — which asserted flatly that none exists. What was actually searched and found: Inner West Council's own chamber-and-association directory was fetched, and its entry for Leichhardt is Viva Leichhardt, which publishes no meeting cadence and presents as a precinct revitalisation collective rather than a meeting-based chamber. A directory absence is evidence that no chamber is listed, not proof that none exists. It is not counted as a room. (Context: Marrickville, Leichhardt and Ashfield councils amalgamated into Inner West Council in 2016, which is why the chamber map does not look the way the suburb names suggest.)
  4. ⛔ "Sydney Inner West Small Business Group" is dormant — last event "No Nonsense Networking," 3 September 2019. No upcoming events.
  5. ⛔ Newtown High School of the Performing Arts and Leichhardt Public School publish no P&C page, meeting date or contact at all. Both homepages were fetched. For those two schools there is no public route — which is exactly the finding the previous version's "the three nearest school P&Cs" row concealed by naming three schools in the wrong half of the city instead.

The rooms that publish a speaker route are almost all retiree rooms — and that is the honest tension

Four of the thirty state on their own page that outside speakers feature: Petersham Probus, Ashfield Probus, OWN Newtown, and Cliff Noble — where a free outside-presenter session by a consultant pharmacist was actually booked, through the coordinator's own address. All four are retiree or older-adult rooms. Of the working-age rooms — the run clubs, the P&Cs, the chambers, Toastmasters — not one publishes a guest-speaker route, and room 1, Inner West Referrals, is the single exception in the entire scan.

⛔ head-of-marketing attacked the framing this section first gave that finding, and was right. The first version said "the route is a person, not a form," which quietly recodes missing demand as a difference of contact method. A published speaker route is not a channel. It is evidence that the room wants outside speakers. Its absence in twenty-six of the thirty rooms means MFC has a way to ask and no evidence that anyone in those rooms wants to be asked — and "searching harder will not fix it" converts we don't know into we have established this, when the thing that would resolve it is asking, and zero asks have been made.

⛔ And the discount was applied where it cost least. The first version closed on "it is why §1.1 keeps community X2 at 3%" — but the rooms with published speaker routes are all on the community side, while the professional side carries eleven of the nineteen standings and effectively all the revenue, publishes no speaker route in any of its sixteen rooms, and runs the higher capture assumption (X1 30% against 15%). Five of its eight VERIFIED rooms are Rotary clubs entered from a district-directory line, and rows 7 and 11 concede in their own cells that Rotary's speaker culture is "a general pattern, not this club's published offer."

So, plainly: it applies with more force to the professional funnel, not less. Nothing here resolves it. The first four asks below are the experiment, and the professional X1 of 30% should be treated as unheld until at least two of them come back. It is also why §2.4's "happy to come to one first as a guest" offer matters more in this list than it did in the invented one.

⛔ The 440 Run Club — and the two facts about Hunter that were in the vault the whole time

⛔ This subsection was written once today, adversarially checked, and found to be wrong in its premise. What follows is the corrected version, with the error left visible.

What the first version of this section said: that the 440 was "a committee suggestion (R0, 20 July) that the previous build converted into a standing weekly founder commitment without anyone checking the distance," and that it should be dropped and replaced one-for-one by two Inner West run clubs, because "the same mechanism at a closer club produces the same conversations."

The skeptic pass read the actual 20 July transcript (10-sources/fathom-transcripts/2026-07-20-committee-strategy-session-business-plan.md). Three things in it break that.

1 · The idea was Rob Dooley's; what was Hunter's was the redirect to existing clubs — and an earlier draft of this line got that backwards. ⚠ And Hunter's own reservation, at 41:57, belongs beside it rather than being quietly dropped: "It's an-saturated space. Like, I know there's so many running exec, there's so many things." He said that about this channel, in this session, before agreeing with the redirect.

What survives the correction, and it is the part that matters: the qualifier all three used was executive. ⛔ That is who is standing in the room, not how far away the room is — and it is the distinction the previous build lost when it turned an executive-network idea into a 5am beach run club, and the one this build lost again when it treated distance as the only variable. ⚠ R0 is also cited once in this plan and never defined: a source code with no legend, which is how the attribution went unchecked for two drafts.

2 · ⛔ Hunter already has the relationship, and it is recorded in his own words (45:12): "I have a great connection with 440 Run Club." The vault did record one of the rooms he is already in. Both builds missed it — the first invented a geography around it, and this one dropped it for being far away.

3 · ⛔ And in the same breath, the fact that undoes the substitution (45:12): "I'm not a Run Club person. I'm a cycling club person, right?"

So the honest position is not "swap the club." It is this:
