⛔ K7 — "the room supply is not there" — is NOT closed, and an earlier draft of this section claimed it was. That claim said sixteen professional rooms support eleven standings. It counted listings as supply. A standing requires a yes; a listing is not a slot — and the same section, forty lines above, says MFC "has a way to ask and no evidence that anyone in those rooms wants to be asked." Both cannot be true.

On this section's own strict grade: professional VERIFIED = 8 rooms against 11 professional standings. The 16 clears 11 only by including eight LISTED rooms — among them Newtown Enmore (nothing forward-dated past June 2026), Lions (no meeting day, time or venue published anywhere reachable), and four rooms explicitly not re-fetched today. Community is better placed: 11 VERIFIED against 8 standings.

What is genuinely closed is the invention problem, not the supply problem. Thirty sourced rooms replaced twenty-eight partly-invented ones; the previous version could not have been checked at all. This one can, and it fails the professional half of the check by three rooms. ⚠ The fix is the City of Sydney sweep already named above, and it is one afternoon.

The standing-slot target survives, and it inverts — and the inversion matters more than the count. ⛔ There is no monthly community slot on this list, and head-of-community proved it from the source rather than asserting it. Fort Street P&C's four published 2026 agendas are fixed and internal — President's welcome, SRC, Secretary, Principal, Treasurer, IMP, General Business — and grepping all four for guest / speaker / presenter / invited returns no external-presenter item in any month. A P&C grants one guest item, because there is nothing for MFC to do at the next meeting. A Probus club books a speaker every month and specifically wants a different one; that is the format, not a failure of the ask.

A repeat presence is available in exactly the rooms that cannot give a slot — a Men's Shed at morning tea, a facilitated neighbourhood-centre group like Glebe Men's Connection, a run club — where after the first yes nobody has to agenda anything.

So: two professional standing slots and one quarterly by month three, plus one repeat community presence counted at ~4 conversations a visit rather than at a room size — the room-13 mechanism, applied a second time. ⚑ This still cuts the sourcing requirement to roughly fifteen yeses rather than thirty-one. It just no longer pretends a P&C will book MFC eight times. ⚑ A room MFC returns to monthly is a community MFC participates in without convening one — and that, not a mailbox, is the honest answer to C3.

⚑ The travel assumption, re-checked from the Inner West

§1.3 allows 1.75 hours per on-site date — travel, the fifteen minutes, and twenty minutes afterwards — which implies about an hour of travel. From the Inner West: Rozelle, Marrickville, Petersham, Annandale, Ashfield, Newtown, Haberfield and Enmore are all inside fifteen minutes. Abbotsford, Concord, Five Dock, Drummoyne and Burwood are fifteen to twenty-five. The CBD is twenty to thirty by train. The assumption holds, and it is now conservative rather than optimistic — the previous list required a 5am round trip to Bronte and a chamber in Ku-ring-gai.

⛔ No hours are reclaimed on the strength of this. The saving is real and it is deliberately not banked: the plan already carries a five-hour headline it cannot meet, and taking credit for shorter drives would be exactly the arithmetic that made the 5.00 fiction in the first place. It is recorded as slack, not as budget.

The first month, concretely

⛔ The previous version opened with "rooms 1, 2, 3 and 4 need no outreach at all — they are relationships that already exist." That sentence is withdrawn. Three of those four were Eastern Suburbs rooms and the fourth was not a gathering. The honest position is that this plan starts with cold outreach, because the vault does not record a single Inner West room Hunter is already in.

The four room asks and two partner asks that the 0.75 sourcing budget buys in weeks 1–2, chosen for warmth and timing rather than for size:

  1. Inner West Referrals (room 1) — the only room whose own page publishes a visiting-expert slot, and the first meeting is free, so the guest-first visit is their process rather than a favour.
  2. ASHbiz (room 3) — the Business Breakfast is 29 September, three weeks out, and the page says events other than board meetings are open to members and non-members. ⚠ Confirm whether non-member attendance is free or ticketed when you enquire — the page does not say.
  3. Fort Street High School P&C (room 15) — a published email, a published agenda process, working-age parents, and demonstrable currency in its own 2026 minutes.
  4. Petersham Probus (room 23) — a named person, a published address, and an explicit standing guest-speaker slot. The easiest yes on the list, and honestly the least likely to produce a door.
  5. Partner ask 1: AHRI's health, safety and wellbeing network (channel 2).
  6. Partner ask 2: CCWT / ACWA (channel 2).

⚑ Ask room 23 for a repeat booking and room 15 for one agenda item — and the difference is evidenced, not stylistic. Fort Street's own 2026 minutes show a fixed internal agenda with no external presenter in any meeting, and three of the four were held wholly or partly online. A P&C gives one item; a Probus club books a speaker monthly and wants a different one each time, so the repeat there is a relationship rather than a series. The repeat presence the year's arithmetic actually needs comes from a shed at morning tea or a run club, not from either of these — see the standing-slot note above.

⚑ One of the first four is an explicit pilot with a debrief, not a counted standing (§1.3's two rehearsal hours). Room 23 is the right pilot: monthly, forgiving, and the lowest-stakes room on the list in which to get the timing wrong.

2.3 · E3 — what MFC leaves with

The mechanism: the person messages MFC one word from their own phone. MFC captures nothing.

Why this shape and not a QR code to a form. A QR code needs a page — and the live site has no product page and /radar is a 404. A form needs a collection notice and a privacy position MFC does not have. A paper sign-up sheet is the worst of the three: MFC transcribes personal information it cannot yet justify holding (AG-016, Debra: "you've got to have a justification for holding on to someone's personal information"). An inbound message is the only contact that arrives with its own consent already attached, needs no website, no wifi and no page, and works from a phone in a park.

⚑ The keyword is per channel, not one word for everything

head-of-digital's finding: the plan invented a capture mechanism that is a word, then used the same word everywhere — so a chamber breakfast and a Men's Shed were indistinguishable in the inbox, and how_found (free text, answered by the person who remembers least) was the only attribution MFC had. Zero cost to fix; it is a decision, not a build. head-of-product independently found the same word was welded to the desk scenario, which only runs in professional rooms. Both are answered by the same change:

Channel Word Why
Professional rooms WEDNESDAY The scenario the room has just spent three minutes inside. A memory of the content, not a code
Community rooms ATTEMPT ⚑ Those rooms run a different scenario, so WEDNESDAY would mean nothing. ATTEMPT is room-independent and rehearses the one sentence that must survive to Thursday
Social and inbound NOTICE Separates self-sourced inbound from room-sourced in the sheet

What he says, exactly. ⚑ The photo comes first.

"Two things and then I'm out of your way. One — take a photo of this, now, while I'm standing here." (Holds up the printed A5 card. Waits. Actually waits.) "Photos are free and you can delete it in the car.

Two — the one-pager with this rep on it is in the highlight on that account called THE REP. You don't need to message me at all. If you'd rather I sent it to you, or you look after a team, message me the word [WEDNESDAY / ATTEMPT]. That's the only thing I'll ever send you. If you want something after that you can ask, and if you don't, you'll never hear from me again."

Two changes from the first draft, on head-of-marketing's H2 finding (§7.1 M3):

  • Photo first, message second. Almost nobody sends a message to a stranger while standing in a room. Almost everybody will take a photo when told to, because it costs nothing and commits to nothing. The photo is the act that survives the room; the message happens that night on the couch. Asking for the message alone asks for the hardest act at the least likely moment.
  • The word is WEDNESDAY, not REP. REP is MFC's internal vocabulary and, said aloud to a run club, is a gym word. WEDNESDAY is the scenario the room has just spent three minutes inside — twenty to four on a Wednesday — so the word is a memory of the content rather than a code, and it will still mean something to a person opening Instagram nine hours later. It is also unambiguous in a DM inbox, which REP is not.

Said twice — once here, once to anyone who comes up afterwards. And the handle is said aloud twice, slowly, because the card is not legible past the front rows of a 200-person run club.

⚑ The THE REP highlight, and its honest tradeoff

head-of-digital's design change, adopted: put the one-pager in a public Story Highlight so the low-intent majority get the content with no DM at all.

  • Gain: the people who do message are self-selected higher intent — which is exactly where X2 (can they open a door) is weakest. It also protects the 0.75-hour follow-up block at the volumes X1 assumes, and it means a person who sends the word gets the thing in four seconds while the human reply becomes a conversation rather than a delivery.
  • Cost, stated: X1 falls and X2 should rise. Some people who would have messaged for the one-pager now simply take it and are never contacts.
  • Whether that is a better product is an empirical question the first three rooms answer. Record both numbers separately from room 1 so the tradeoff is measured rather than argued.
  • Cost to build: ~1 hour, once. No page, no privacy determination, works offline-later.

Three failure points in this mechanism, two free to fix

  1. ⛔ Instagram Message Requests do not notify. A DM from someone who does not follow the account lands in the Requests tab, raises no badge on the main inbox, and can sit for a fortnight. This is precisely the silent failure §1.3 protects the follow-up hour against, and the first draft did not name it. Fix: a written line in the Thursday block — open Message Requests first. Free.
  2. Nothing acknowledges receipt for up to two business days. Someone who messages a charity at 9pm and hears nothing for 48 hours assumes it did not arrive; their next action is not a second message. Use Instagram's own native instant-reply — "Got it — Hunter replies personally, usually within two days." Native only: ~15 minutes to configure and it adds no processor. ⛔ Do not use ManyChat or any third-party DM tool — that puts a processor between MFC and its contacts with no privacy determination in place. ⚠ Verify the current feature name and limits before relying on it; Meta renames these regularly. (This is not the §4.4 automation: it acknowledges receipt, it does not answer, and it fires on every first message rather than on sentiment.)
  3. ⚠ The Men's Shed fallback published Hunter's personal mobile number to a room of strangers, permanently. Once photographed it cannot be recalled, and the account will — as this plan itself says — eventually receive a distressed message. Fix: a second number used for MFC only, or drop the fallback and hand those rooms the printed card. ⚑ This touches founder safety and is FOUNDER QUESTION 9 — it is not a tooling decision.

⚠ One thing this change does not alter, and head-of-compliance confirmed it (§7.1 C1): the person still initiates the message, and the photograph is of MFC's own public handle. MFC collects nothing in the room and holds nothing until a person chooses to write to it. The consent property that made this mechanism work without a privacy determination is unchanged.

What happens next, and the two-business-day rule:

When What
They message the word Hunter replies personally, within two business days. Not an automation. At this volume (~15 contacts a month) an automation is engineering for a problem MFC does not have, and it carries the failure named in STRATEGY-2026-08-24 §5: a machine replying to "I've been struggling since my brother died" with a link.
The reply The one-page (the rep, written; what MFC is and isn't; crisis lines; nothing else). Plus one sentence: "Out of interest, do you look after other people at work? If your team would ever get something from this in a room, tell me and I'll explain what that looks like — and if not, genuinely, enjoy the one-page."
The record Name, handle, the room, the date, and their answer to that one question, into the one sheet (§6). Nothing else.

Two things stated on the account's profile, permanently, because a mental-fitness account receiving DMs will eventually receive a distressed one: that MFC does not monitor comments or DMs for distress and cannot respond to a crisis, and the crisis lines. This is not optional and it is cheap.

Fallback if Instagram is the wrong channel for that room (a Men's Shed, a room of people over 60): the printed card carries an MFC-only mobile number and the word ATTEMPT. A text message is the same mechanism with the same consent properties — but see failure point 3: it must not be Hunter's personal number.

2.4 · E4 — what the gathering gets

The sentence an organiser would repeat to their committee:

"He'll give your group fifteen minutes on one practical thing they can use this week — it's free, he's a charity, and he's not selling anything to your members."

Three parts and each is load-bearing (AG-020, AG-026 — what's in it for me, and what I could lose):

Part Why it is in the sentence
"fifteen minutes on one practical thing" An organiser's real problem is filling a slot with something that is not boring. This is a specific, small, low-risk answer — not "a talk about mental health," which is the thing they have been offered before and which their members do not turn up for.
"it's free, he's a charity" Removes the price question and the is this a pitch question at once.
"he's not selling anything to your members" The loss side, and it is the one that gets the yes. The organiser's real risk is looking like they let a salesman into their community. This sentence is the answer, and it is true — MFC sells to organisations, not to the individuals in a run club. He should say it unprompted.

What the organiser gets that they can see afterwards: their members talk about it. That is the whole of it, and overclaiming beyond it would be the mistake. Not offered: attendance data, a report, co-branding, or anything that turns a favour into an administrative task for them.

The ask, as an actual email. Subject line included, because the subject line is the ask.

Subject: Fifteen minutes for one of your [breakfasts]?

Hi [name],

I'm in [Marrickville] and I run a small mental-health charity. I teach one practical skill — catching a moment while it's happening and choosing what you do next — in fifteen minutes, standing up, no slides, no handouts.

It's free, and I'm not selling anything to your members. I sell to organisations, not to people in a room.

Would you have fifteen minutes at one of your [breakfasts] in the next couple of months? Happy to come to one first as a guest so you can see who you'd be putting in front of your people.

Hunter

Four things changed from the first draft, on head-of-marketing's H2 finding (§7.1 M1):

Change Why
The subject line is the whole ask. "Fifteen minutes for one of your breakfasts?" An organiser triages a full inbox by subject. "Mental fitness charity" in a subject line gets filed; a fifteen-minute slot request is a scheduling question they already know how to answer
"I sell to organisations, not to people in a room" replaces "people generally like it" The first is the answer to their actual fear. The second is an unevidenced claim about a thing MFC has never done — MFC has zero delivered rooms, so "people generally like it" is not true yet and must not be written
The offer to attend once as a guest first Removes the organiser's real risk — putting an unknown person in front of their members sight-unseen — at a cost of one evening. It is also how rooms 2, 3 and 18 will actually be entered
Square-bracket the format word, not three options "[Tuesday nights / breakfasts / meetings]" reads as a template. One word, matched to that room, reads as a person who looked them up
⚑ 2026-09-05: locality goes in, and it goes first — "I'm in [Marrickville]" replaces "in Sydney" head-of-marketing's ruling on the Inner West rebuild. "In Sydney" carries zero information to a Marrickville chamber secretary — every organisation that will ever email her is in Sydney, so it is filler in the most-read position. Locality is the strongest asset MFC has in these rooms: no delivery record, no evidence, no name recognition, nothing to charge — what it has is local, free, charity, and only two of those three were in the first line. ⚠ It is attached to Hunter, not to MFC, deliberately: the vault records no registered address for MFC and a reader can check the ACNC register. And it is bracketed for the same reason the format word is — nine of the thirty rooms are not Inner West, and to Vistage's inbox "the Inner West" is a shrink, not a credential. Take the suburb where MFC and the room share one, the region where they share the region, "Sydney" for rooms 26–29
⛔ REFUSED: dropping "mental-health" from "a small mental-health charity" head-of-marketing argued, reasonably, that §2.4's own table says "a talk about mental health" is the thing organisers have been offered before and their members do not turn up for — so leading with the category triggers the "we did that, nobody came" reflex before the specific offer lands, and "I run a small charity" lets the concrete skill do the identifying. Refused, on a risk it did not weigh: an organiser who says yes to "a small charity" and later discovers it is a mental-health charity has been managed rather than told. The whole ask rests on the organiser not feeling they let something in sight-unseen, and §2.4 says so in its own third row. A slightly harder first line is the price of a true one

2.4a · The one positioning sentence, for professional rooms only

In the professional rooms — a chamber, Rotary, a business network — one extra sentence goes in at the close, because that room contains people who buy training and who have sat through the category before. It is not said in a community room, where it would sound like a pitch, and it is cut first under time pressure (§2.1 cut 4).

"Most training in this space is built to hand people content. What the transfer literature points at is conditions and practice, not more content — and I'll be straight with you, we've never delivered a measured cohort either, so we've got no evidence of our own to wave at you. What we build is the other half: the thing that makes the reps actually happen. That's what we do with an organisation over more than one sitting, and it's what I'd talk to you about if you ever wanted it."

⛔ Rewritten by head-of-compliance (§7.1 C3), and the first draft's version was a real breach. It read "Most training in this space tells people what to do. The reason it doesn't stick isn't that people didn't listen..." — and the plan asserted that this made no negative effectiveness claim about anyone else. It does. "It doesn't stick" is a general statement that a category does not work, with a causal explanation attached, delivered unpaired, to the rooms with the money in them. P37 permits a comparative evidence claim only when the same sentence carries MFC's own limit, and never as a general statement that a category "doesn't work." D-0104 and ACL s18 both sit on it. The replacement pairs the limit in-sentence, which is what P37 requires — and the pairing is one sentence, never cut for length.

⚑ Also refused: head-of-marketing offered a rhetorical framing about the number of small moments in a life. REFUSED FOR SPOKEN USE (§7.1). No number describing moments, weeks or people is ever spoken in a room — §2.5's never-say list bars any statistic, and a rhetorical figure is indistinguishable from a claimed one once it has been said aloud with no leave-behind to check it against.

2.5 · E5 — claims safety in a room with no leave-behind

A fifteen-minute teach to strangers with nothing in writing is the highest-risk claims surface MFC has, because there is no page to check the sentence against afterwards and no facilitator's note. So the boundary is carried in the run sheet itself, not in a document.

Permitted, and used above — P1, P2, P4, P10(c), P13, P14, P19, P31, P34, P36. Every sentence in §2.1 traces to one of these or to the workshop's own P3a–P7 script.

The three that must be said, in every version including the five-minute one:

Must be said Which permitted sentence
"I'm not a psychologist. This is education and skills practice, not therapy or crisis support." P1, P31
"It's a capacity, not perfection. You will miss moments." P4 — protective, not a disclaimer. Never said as fine print at the end
"A skill doesn't replace a bad environment. If the conditions are genuinely against you, this is not the answer to that." P14 — the falsifiable boundary, and what makes the rest credible

Never said, in any room, including in answer to a question:

  • "This reduces stress / burnout / anxiety" — or any of them softened to "helps with."
  • "Evidence-based", "backed by research", "proven", "validated." "Evidence-informed" and no stronger words.
  • "Rewires your brain", "neuroplasticity", "builds new pathways."
  • Any statistic. Any at all — no "one in five," no "most managers," no "$X billion in lost productivity." No number reaches a room without a registered 10-sources/ record, and the "3,000 people asked / 78% self-taught" figures specifically have no log, no denominator and no fixed wording behind them and must not be spoken.
  • "Prevents mental illness" or any prevention framing. "Reduces suicide risk" — no exception, including where the founder story would make it tempting. This one is named because in these rooms someone will ask him why he started MFC, and the honest answer runs directly at it.
  • "You will..." anything. Can, not will — the single most common way a permitted sentence gets strengthened in a live room, and fifteen standing-up minutes is exactly where it happens.

The three prepared answers he carries, because these questions will be asked in a room and there is no leave-behind to point at:

They ask He says
"Does this actually work?" "We've never delivered a measured cohort. We've got no results to show you and we're not going to invent any. What research validates is the mechanisms we've built on — that's a different claim, and you should hold us to the difference. Proof will come from measurement across cohorts or it won't come at all, and we'll publish either way." (P19, P21, P20)
"My friend is really struggling — what should they do?" "I'm not the right person for that and this isn't the right thing for it. Lifeline is 13 11 14, twenty-four hours, and a GP is a genuinely good first step. Mental fitness complements professional care; it doesn't replace it." (P17) Then he stops. He does not offer to talk to them.
⚑ "That's me. That's what I'm going through." — said quietly, to him, afterwards "Thanks for telling me — that's not a small thing to say to someone you just met. I'm not a professional and this isn't the thing for that, so I'm not going to pretend otherwise. Lifeline's 13 11 14, any time, and they're good. A GP is a genuinely good first step too. Is there someone in your life who knows?" Then he stops, he does not follow up, and he writes nothing down. ⚑ Added on head-of-compliance's finding (§7.1 C4): the first draft scripted "my friend is struggling" and not "that's me" — the more likely of the two after a room like this. AG-015: he stays fifteen minutes after every room, which is when this happens
"Isn't this just resilience training / positive thinking?" "No — and I'd say the same about the ones that are. This isn't about trying harder or thinking positively. It's one loop for ordinary moments: notice a moment while it's happening, change one thing so it's workable, choose the next move on purpose toward what matters. It's practised inside the life you're already living. We're not asking anyone for another fifteen minutes a day." (P36, P12)

One structural note flagged rather than solved: 11-PERMITTED-CLAIMS.md states its own limit — K-08's red lines are themselves unapproved (BD-03 pending), so this section makes MFC's position legible and checkable; it does not make it approved. That is the same limit the paid products carry.

3 · THE AMBASSADOR PROGRAMME (A)

Finalised from 90-meta/PROGRAMME-2026-08-25-ambassador-v1.html and the Tom debrief. Nothing about Tom is public, nothing is agreed, and this plan changes neither.

3.1 · A1 — what is in it for Tom, honestly

MFC cannot pay. So the offer has to be something a person with 180,000 followers cannot easily buy, and it has to be stated in his terms.

What he gets Why it is actually worth something to him, not to MFC
An answer to the thing he raised himself He said, unprompted, "who am I to share these things?" and that all he can really do is tell men to go to therapy — and that mostly only women reply. The rep model solves his stated problem: he stops explaining mental health, which is the role a qualification is required for, and starts reporting what he did, which needs no credential and cannot be contradicted by a stranger. That is a permanent upgrade to his own content, and it is his whether or not the MFC relationship continues.
He names the reps. If he calls it "three strides," MFC's material calls it three strides — internally, in front of the committee, everywhere. Given away before he asks. Cheapest and most powerful item on this list.
An unsolved problem, not a finished asset The running version of a Mental Rep does not exist and Hunter is not a runner. Tom is. He is the author, and MFC adopts his version. A polished concept makes him a distributor; a problem makes him an author.
A real decision with a visible consequence Which archetype MFC approaches next, decided by the ambassador group, acted on within four weeks, and reported back at the next session.
Being trusted with the weaknesses Zero workshops delivered, what MFC can't claim, what didn't land last year — shown to him. This is the whole difference between "I'm part of something" and "I'm helping a mate."
A protected relationship with his own audience The guardrails in the draft §6 are framed to him as protecting his career, because they do. Several men's-wellbeing creators have run out of past and started spending present; the four-in-five-ordinary-moments rule removes that gradient entirely.
A structured exit Fixed 12-month term, one text to step down, no reason required. Turns "how do I end this?" into a scheduled no-fault conversation.

Is it enough? Honestly: it is enough for the first term and it is not obviously enough for a second. The offer is built almost entirely from authorship and trust, which are strong at the start and depreciate. What makes a second term worth it is evidence that his reps reached people — which MFC will not have until it measures, which is the same dependency everything else in MFC has. Stated here rather than assumed away. The floor is deliberately three hours a year, so that if the answer is "not enough," he has lost three hours and a friendship survives it.

3.2 · A2 — what MFC gets, measured

Reach is not a lead, and follower counts are not results.

⛔ The first draft made sends on posts the primary monthly number, and that was wrong in two ways the heads caught independently.

  • head-of-community (§7.1): the programme's founding decision is don't ask ambassadors for posts. A metric reported monthly is a quota with better manners — it is the month-four obligation drift, arriving through the back door, and it is the exact outcome Hunter told Tom he did not want.
  • head-of-digital (§7.1 D1): §3.2 set a lead target of "1 inbound message a month naming a rep" while §3.3 imposed no call to action to MFC at all for the first six months. With no CTA, no link, no keyword and no page, there is no mechanism by which that message can be caused or identified. The target and the rule could not both stand.

Both are resolved the same way: measure what the floor actually asks for.

What counts The number How it is recorded
Judgement given — an honest read on something before it went out Count per quarter. Target: any The monthly session recap
One door a year — an introduction to a room MFC cannot get into 1 per ambassador per year The one sheet, channel = ambassador
One person brought to a free session or a room 1 per ambassador per year The one sheet
Sessions attended (floor is two a year; four is a good year) 2–4 The recap
Sends and saves on any post he chooses to make ⚑ A diagnostic Hunter looks at with Tom, never MFC's reported number and never a target Instagram native
⛔ Likes, comments, follower growth Not reported to Tom and not on the monthly page —

Why sends survive at all, demoted. A man forwarding this to another man with no commentary is the behaviour that says it landed, and Instagram reports the gender split on sends natively — which makes it the cheapest possible test of Tom's own diagnosis that only women reply to him. That is worth looking at together. It is not worth turning into a monthly number he has to produce.

And his most-liked post will almost certainly be his most-disclosing one. That is the ratchet firing, not feedback — which is why engagement is not reported to him at all.

Attribution, honestly: ⚑ the third keyword (NOTICE, §2.3) gives ambassador- and social-sourced contacts their own identifiable route without any CTA to MFC being required of Tom — a person who finds MFC because of him arrives through MFC's own channels, not his. how_found remains the cross-check, not the only record.

3.3 · A3 — what he can say and never say, and the DMs

§7 and §8 of the programme draft are finalised below, reviewed by head-of-compliance as a real call — see §7.2 for what that review changed.

The one line, said as part of how he talks, not as a disclaimer at the bottom:

"I'm not a professional. I'm sharing what I did."

He can say: what happened to him; what he did about it; whether it helped him and how much, including "it helped a bit" and "it made the next ten minutes survivable"; that mental fitness is something you practise like physical fitness and you'll still have bad days; that professional help exists and is a good thing; that he's an MFC ambassador — every time.

He must never say: anything that sounds like a diagnosis, his or anyone else's; that this treats, prevents, cures or fixes mental illness, burnout or anything else; that it replaces therapy, a psychologist, a GP or a crisis line; "this rewires your brain"; "this will work for you"; that anyone can shift any moment if they try hard enough; anything describing a suicide attempt or method; advice in DMs about someone else's mental health; ⚑ any claim about MFC's products, evidence or outcomes — his lane is strictly first-person, and if he is asked what MFC's workshops do, the answer is "ask Hunter"; ⚑ anything quoting or describing a message he has received; and ⚑ any fundraising ask (see below).

⚑ One factual correction, and it matters because a compliance page that gets a legal fact wrong is the thing that costs credibility with a law firm. The first draft said psychologist and counsellor "are legally protected titles in Australia." "Psychologist" is protected under the Health Practitioner Regulation National Law and using it unregistered is an offence. "Counsellor" is not a protected title in Australia — it is unregulated. The correct wording for Tom: "'Psychologist' is a protected title here and using it without registration is against the law. 'Counsellor' isn't protected, but implying you're one is still misleading, and it's out."

The three tests, for when he is unsure: Am I talking about me, or telling them about them? Am I promising an outcome? Would this be fine if a journalist screenshot it next to MFC's logo?

And a standing right to send anything to Hunter before posting, with no obligation to explain why.

The post that would end the programme — ⚑ and what actually happens if one goes up

⛔ "Programme-ending for one post" was itself a defect (§7.1 C6). As written it guarantees Tom hides a borderline post rather than showing Hunter — the same incentive logic §6's ratchet guardrail is built on, inverted. Corrected: one post over the line is a conversation and a deletion. A pattern is the exit.

The incident response, written before it is needed: Tom deletes it himself; MFC does not repost, quote or screenshot it; Hunter tells the committee within 48 hours; and MFC issues no public statement without Eimear Quigley and Bird & Bird.

Shown to him deliberately, because it is much easier to see the line from the wrong side of it:

"I used to have really bad anxiety. Doctors just want to medicate you. Then I found mental fitness and honestly it rewired my brain — three weeks of daily reps and the anxiety was gone. If you're struggling you don't need therapy, you need to train your mind like you train your body. DM me and I'll walk you through it."

Six breaches in one caption: self-diagnosis as fact, undermining professional care, "rewired my brain," a cure claim with a timeline, "you don't need therapy," and offering to personally coach strangers in DMs.

The DMs — the thing that will actually happen

A creator posting about mental fitness will get crisis DMs. Not a risk — a certainty. MFC has no clinicians. Design principle: Tom is never the responder. He is a signpost with a script, and the script has to work at 11pm on his phone when he is tired.

Three saved replies, pre-loaded in Instagram's saved-replies before his first post, so he taps rather than composes:

1 · Someone is struggling

"Really glad you told me, and I'm sorry you're in it. I'm not a professional so I can't advise you — but these people are, they're free, and they're good: Lifeline 13 11 14 or text 0477 13 11 14, 24/7. Beyond Blue 1300 22 4636. If you're under 25, Kids Helpline 1800 55 1800. Please reach out to one of them, or your GP. Take care of yourself."

2 · Immediate danger

"I'm really worried about you and I'm not the right person to help with this — you deserve someone who actually knows what they're doing. Please call 000 if you're in danger right now, or Lifeline on 13 11 14, they're there 24/7. I'm going to step back from this chat because I can't give you what you need, but I do want you to be OK."

Then: send it, don't wait for a reply, close the app, and message Hunter. Not because Hunter can fix it — because Tom should not be alone with it. That is the actual function.

3 · "Will you be my coach?"

"I appreciate that, genuinely. But I'm not a professional and it wouldn't be fair to either of us for me to take that on. Everything I've got is in the posts — help yourself to all of it."

⛔ THE TRIGGER RULE — rewritten by head-of-compliance, and this was the biggest single defect in the plan (§7.1 C1). The first draft said "never assess how serious it is" and then handed Tom three replies and no rule for choosing. Choosing between reply 1 and reply 2 IS an assessment of severity. At 11pm, tired, on his phone, that is where the design fails — and it is the paragraph that gets read out if this ever goes wrong.

"You never judge how bad it is — you're not qualified to and you don't have to be. Reply 1 is what you send. Always. The only exception is if they have told you, in their own words, that they are going to hurt themselves now or tonight — then it's reply 2. You're not reading between the lines. If you're not sure, it's reply 1, and reply 1 is never the wrong answer."

That converts a clinical judgement into a keyword match, which is what a non-clinician can actually do under load.

⚑ Fourth saved reply — someone worried about another person. After "I'm struggling," this is the most likely DM a men's-wellbeing account receives, and the first draft had no script for it:

"That's a really hard thing to be carrying, and it says a lot that you're worried. I'm not a professional so I can't advise you — but Lifeline (13 11 14) will talk to you about how to help someone else, not just about you, and that's genuinely what they're there for. 13YARN is 13 92 76 if you're Aboriginal or Torres Strait Islander. If you think they're in danger right now, call 000. Look after yourself in this too."

⚑ 13YARN (13 92 76) is added to replies 1 and 4. It is the Australian standard on every crisis surface now and its absence was conspicuous.

⚑ Reply 2's closing is changed. "I'm going to step back from this chat" is a correct boundary in the worst possible sentence — at 11pm, to someone who has just disclosed intent, it reads as a door closing. Replace the last two clauses with: "...Lifeline on 13 11 14, they're there 24/7. I can't keep talking about this because I'm not qualified and I'd get it wrong — but I mean this, I want you to be OK. Please make that call." Same boundary, no announced withdrawal. ⚠ This exact wording is the one item head-of-compliance would not sign as settled — it is Mindframe's call, and it is what the free call is for.

Three rules to memorise: never move to a private ongoing conversation with someone in distress; apply the trigger rule above rather than judging severity; and he can leave any conversation at any point. Leaving is the correct action, not abandonment.

⚑ And "Tom is never the responder" was not actually implemented. As first drafted he was the sole responder — reading, classifying, replying, and telling Hunter afterwards. Two of the three things that make the principle real were missing:

  • A line in his bio saying he does not answer DMs about how someone is going. §2.3 requires exactly this on MFC's own 8,800 account and the first draft omitted it from the 180,000 one — an asymmetry that is backwards. Add: "I share what I do, I don't advise, and I can't reply to messages about how you're going — Lifeline 13 11 14, 24/7."
  • ⛔ He never quotes, screenshots, reposts or describes a DM he receives — even anonymised, even to make a good point. This was missing entirely and it is the single most common serious harm creators cause in this space.

Plus: crisis numbers in his bio or a pinned post, so a distressed follower hits a pathway without needing him to be awake. Keyword comment-filters on heavier posts. Never answer a critical comment the same night. A monthly two-question check, five minutes, informal: "anything you posted this month you'd rather hadn't gone up?" and "is this still fun?" — the second one catches the ratchet.

One qualified set of eyes on the crisis wording before it is used. Mindframe (Everymind) exists in Australia specifically to advise on public communication about suicide and mental ill-health, and it is free. That is a phone call, not a consultancy fee, and it is the only real assurance in a system that has no clinicians in it. It is a pre-first-post gate, below.

3.4 · A4 — the first four weeks contain no Tom post

Nothing public. Tom is not named anywhere. No post until an agreement exists and the pre-first-post items are done.

Week What happens What does not happen
1–2 Write the two-page pack, not ten. Twenty minutes of ordinary due diligence on Tom — it will be clean, and doing it establishes that the process is real for ambassadors two and three. Map the other audiences and name candidates without approaching anyone. One line to the committee: first ambassador is a personal friend of the founder, terms uniform, no financial interest. Draft month one's decision question. No post. No mention of Tom to anyone outside MFC. Nothing on any MFC channel referencing an ambassador programme.
2–3 Send Tom the pack. Two things the message must do: name the three gaps explicitly and ask him to think about them, and say the once-only line — "I'd rather you say no and we stay exactly as we are than say yes because it's me." Then do not follow up. Let him arrive having had time. No post. No follow-up nudge.
3–4 The coffee. Ninety minutes. Arrive with a draft, leave with a decision — a real yes, or a clean no given after a week's thought, received warmly. The three gaps filled, his "not sharing" list agreed, the term and exit understood, the first monthly session in the calendar. If he hedges, that is a no. No post.

Before his first MFC post — all four, all cheap: 1. The three saved replies actually loaded on his phone. 2. His "not sharing" list written down, and MFC never asking him past it. 3. The two-page agreement signed, including that he can stop any time by text, no notice, no reason. 4. One qualified set of eyes on the crisis wording — Mindframe.

⚑ Ambassador, not spokesperson — reinstated

The first draft silently dropped the programme draft's §9 recommendation, and it is the control standing between MFC and a claim its professional-liability exclusion would not answer: run this as an ambassadorship with "in my own words" framing for the first six months, then review with the committee once there is actual evidence of how it is going. "I back this, and here's my own experience" keeps Tom's statements his own; a face of the charity makes MFC answerable for them and exposes MFC to his entire public life, past and future. It costs nothing, keeps every door open, and does not change a word of what Hunter says to Tom.

head-of-compliance's call on §3.3

SIGNED OFF WITH CONDITIONS.

The architecture is right and no more apparatus is being asked for. Tom-as-reporter-not-explainer, the can-say/never-say split, the fixed term, the one-text exit, the boring-posts-are-the-product guardrail and "I'm not a professional, I'm sharing what I did" are a better ambassador design than most funded charities run. Eimear's standing correction applies — separate from clinical risk by disclaimer, not by apparatus — and this does that.

The nine conditions, all pre-first-post, all cheap, all applied above except where noted: the rewritten trigger rule; the fourth saved reply; 13YARN in replies 1 and 4; the bio non-response line; the two added never-say lines; the protected-title correction; the incident response replacing programme-ending; the ambassadorship framing reinstated; the broker email sent with two added questions (below); and Mindframe reviewing reply 2's wording specifically.

⛔ None of these blocks A4's first four weeks. The pack, the due diligence, the coffee and the agreement all proceed.

⚠ AI review is never human assurance. Still required from a qualified human: Eimear Quigley on reply 2's wording and the in-room disclosure response; Mindframe (Everymind), free, one phone call, on the crisis-DM wording; Bird & Bird on the exit and content-licence clauses and the ambassadorship framing; and Osman Insurance Brokers on everything below.

⚑ One thing head-of-compliance could not license and named rather than softened: whether an ambassador with a 180,000 audience soliciting or receiving donations for MFC engages the Charitable Fundraising Act 1991 (NSW) third-party fundraiser provisions. MFC has raised money publicly before, so an authority may exist, but it could not be verified in the vault and the plan did not contemplate the question at all. If Tom ever says "chip in," that becomes live. "No fundraising asks" is on the never-say list until the committee confirms whether MFC holds a current NSW fundraising authority — FOUNDER QUESTION 10.

Insurance — what is outside cover, and the one email

⚑ The email already exists and has not been sent. 50-authority/insurance-broker-brief-2026-09-01.md — Jake to Michael Sussmann at Osman, drafted 1 September, still draft-for-sending.

Outside cover Detail
Anything the teaching itself could be blamed for BUP026 (Total Professional Liability) and BUP022 (Treatment) remove any claim "connected with the rendering of… professional advice or service." The $20m public liability answers someone tripping over a cable. It does not answer a claim that fifteen unscreened minutes harmed someone. A standing MFC gap, not a new one — but §2 puts ~485 unscreened strangers through it this year
The declaration does not describe this activity The existing brief asks about paid delivery in third-party workplaces. §2 is free public speaking at third-party premises, ~31 times a year; §3 is an unpaid public representative with a 180,000 audience. Neither is in the declaration
⚑ BUP016 (Molestation) and Vero's ABUSE EXCLUSION 0923 Nobody had named this. MFC is attaching its name to a person in one-to-one DM contact with a large audience that will include vulnerable people and minors. That surface is excluded from both policies in full. Not a reason to stop — it is the reason "never move to a private ongoing conversation with someone in distress" is a control rather than etiquette, and it belongs in the signed two-page agreement, not only in the pack
Volunteer personal accident Not held, not found. Tom is a volunteer
The certificate of currency does not exist Despite a July 2025 committee action. A corporate room will ask for it

Two questions to add to that email before it goes: (6) whether any policy responds to a claim arising from a volunteer representative's public statements — defamation, misleading conduct, or a claim by a member of the public who contacted them — and the position under BUP016 and ABUSE EXCLUSION 0923 where that contact is one-to-one direct messaging; (7) whether ~31 unpaid fifteen-minute talks a year at third-party premises are within the declared activity and answered by the $20m public liability.

Two items that need a human, not this plan: whether MFC's insurance covers a volunteer representative's public statements (written broker confirmation, one email), and the wording of the exit and content-licence clauses. On exit: MFC stops reusing his story in decks, funder material and website copy within 30 days and confirms in writing when done. He owns his story throughout.

3.5 · A5 — who else, and why

Hunter's ruling stands: Tom does not recruit. Friend-of-friend recruitment produces a socially identical group and makes declining awkward for everybody. MFC finds them, deliberately, across audiences it otherwise will not reach. Map the audiences first, then find the people.

Three to start, not five. Tom now; one more by month six (see below); hold for a full term. A programme that starts at five and fails, fails publicly with five people who have audiences.

⚑ Before recruiting anyone cold: MFC already has 30–40 ambassadors and 350+ signups

The first draft proposed finding ambassadors two and three by cold search through clubs and sheds. That is a recruitment problem MFC solved a year ago and forgot. Mental Fitness Month produced 350+ signups and 30–40 ambassadors — verified and publishable (60-projects/mfc-theory-of-change-v1/THEORY-OF-CHANGE.md §5 and §14.6; checked for supersession, no signal). The Theory of Change calls that cohort "the largest carrier cohort MFC will have for a year, and the only real demand-side signal MFC owns — those people volunteered, unpaid, with no product to sell them."

Action, before week three: one message to the existing cohort. One sentence and one ask. Under an hour, not a campaign, and it comes before any cold approach in the archetype table below. Ambassador two is likelier in that group than in a cold search, and the same list is what makes Option D's co-hosted sessions inviteable.

⚠ Two things this plan cannot license and does not assert: whether that list carries usable consent, and whether it is current. That is one login to check — the same failure the Theory of Change §15.1 records about the unopened gap-survey responses. ⛔ And it runs straight into a live registered conflict: the ambassador programme was "parked as reactive-only in June, reactivated by August", and the Theory of Change marks it "not mine to close." That must be closed before the cohort is contacted — FOUNDER QUESTION 8. AG-063 (Onno: ambassadors intrinsically aligned with the cause, not merely popular) is satisfied by the selection criteria below and is cited here rather than left implicit.

The two further archetypes, named, with the route to each

# Archetype Why this one The route
2 The sub-elite or ex-athlete — the one who didn't quite make it. Trained hard, fell short, still runs/trains. NOT an elite athlete. A-22's reversal of Hunter's original idea, and it is right: an elite athlete's moments aren't ordinary, so a viewer can't map "before the grand final" onto their Tuesday; their capability gets attributed to being exceptional rather than to the rep, which destroys the copyable mechanism that is the entire point; and it is the most saturated space in the category. Sub-elite is failure-shaped, which is the tall-poppy-safe form here. Through a club, not through a follower count. Suburban and regional clubs in athletics, rugby league, triathlon and surf life saving have people who trained at a high level, still compete, have a real modest following, and are already in a community MFC wants to be in. Concretely: the community rooms in §2.2 are the sourcing ground — the sub-elite athlete is more likely to be found standing in one of those rooms than searched for online. First step is a name suggested by a club, not a DM to a profile.
3 The tradie or shift worker with a modest real following. 900 to 5,000 followers, not a creator, posts about their actual life. Peer, not authority — hierarchy degrades this message. Their moments are inherently ordinary, which is exactly what the four-in-five-ordinary-moments guardrail requires and what a bigger account struggles to supply. The lowest cost to support and the highest credibility per follower. And it is Hunter's own original audience: he is a former tradie, and the two men he lost in construction are why MFC exists. Through the trade rooms and Men's Sheds in §2.2, and through the construction network Hunter already has. First step: after a room, the person who came up afterwards and said the most useful thing. The best ambassador in this archetype is someone who has already done a rep in a room, not someone recruited off a profile.

Who looks right and will not work, recorded so it is not re-proposed: the therapist or psychologist creator (the instant a qualified person delivers it, it becomes advice and re-enters the clinical register men avoid — and it retrospectively reframes Tom's posts as the amateur version); the big wellness or mindset creator (audience already converted, mostly female, business model is the escalation ratchet); the recovery-story ambassador whose public identity is what they came through (valuable people, wrong role — their content is inherently about the size of the moment); anyone corporate or senior; and nobody from the committee or Hunter's professional network, which turns it into an internal exercise.

⚑ Two corrections from head-of-community (§7.1).

  1. The tradie archetype's sourcing route did not intersect its own spec. "The person who came up after a room" and "900–5,000 followers already posting about their life" is close to an empty set. Invert it: two hours searching Australian tradie and shift-worker accounts in that follower band, then use the room network for the warm introduction. Search for the profile; use the network to reach them.
  2. "Two more by month three" was not costed. Two more means two packs, two due-diligence passes, two agreements, two Mindframe reviews and two more people in the monthly session — inside 0.35 hours a week. Corrected: one more by month six. Tom, then one. The second wave waits for a completed term.

⚑ And look at the list MFC already has before searching. Mental Fitness Month produced 350+ signups and 30–40 people who acted as ambassadors (head-of-movement, MV1). Ambassador two is more likely to be in that group than in a cold search — they have already said yes to MFC once. Subject to a consent and currency check on that list before anybody is contacted, and it does not change the archetypes above; it changes where the search starts. This is also the list §5.3's Option D leans on.

⚑ And the floor contains something MFC cannot currently deliver. "Your name on the wall" is half the obligation an ambassador accepts — and the live site has no page to put it on (§0). Either that one page gets built (§6.3, Alysse's hours, not Hunter's) or the item comes out of the floor. Do not offer a friend a credit MFC cannot render.

Selection ratio, deliberate: one archetype reaching people MFC would otherwise never meet (the tradie), one reaching a distinct audience with a copyable mechanism (the sub-elite athlete), and Tom's existing reach. A woman in a male-dominated setting is the strongest fourth and is held for the second wave rather than added now, because three is the number that survives one person's five hours.

4 · SOCIAL (S)

4.1 · S1 — the evidence question, answered before the cadence is set

Hunter's own framing: "don't want to be too active — although open to it if evidence supports this move/approach." So the evidence decides it, and the honest answer is not the flattering one.

The answer: No. The evidence does not support that posting more produces leads for an organisation in MFC's position. What it does support is a floor — below roughly two to three posts a week, reach measurably degrades. And reach is not the bottleneck MFC has.

What was actually found, for and against

Finding Tier
FOR Buffer's analysis of ~2 million Instagram posts: reach per post rises with frequency, with the biggest jump from 1–2 to 3–5 posts a week and clearly diminishing returns beyond that (~20–25% incremental). EVIDENCE by sample size; vendor-owned data by source. The largest sample found.
FOR Multiple independent vendor sources converge: an account that goes silent underperforms its own baseline. "Any posting beats none." VENDOR CLAIM, directionally corroborated across sources, none independently verified
FOR LinkedIn vendor consensus: 3–5 posts a week is optimal, with diminishing returns past that and a penalty for two same-day posts. VENDOR CLAIM
AGAINST Every source that measures frequency measures it against reach or engagement. Not one measures it against enquiries, conversations or sales. Every source that measures conversion (LinkedIn 2.74%, Instagram ~1%, paid social 0.9%) attributes it to channel and offer specificity, never to volume. The absence is the finding, and it is where the question actually lives
AGAINST Education-sector LinkedIn data: highest engagement at 2–3 posts a week; posting more did not improve it. VENDOR CLAIM, uncited primary — but it points the same way
AGAINST The one operator-level data point found (a small B2C wellness account, ~2 years of its own Insights, N=1): click-through rate, not follower count or frequency, tracked with enquiries. Its author states directly that when results lack enquiries, "the issue is rarely the posting frequency." WEAK — N=1, self-reported, wrong vertical (B2C not B2B). Reported because it is the most operationally relevant thing found, not because it is strong
AGAINST — and this is the structural one Ehrenberg-Bass / LinkedIn B2B Institute: growth comes from penetration — reaching more actual category buyers — not from intensity of exposure to an existing follower base. Marketing-science tier. The strongest reasoning available
NOT FOUND A direct search for evidence that charity or nonprofit organic social converts followers into paid B2B service buyers returned nothing, vendor or academic. Search terms recorded. A real gap, stated rather than filled

Why the structural argument decides it for MFC specifically

MFC's 8,800 Instagram followers are very unlikely to be the people who buy a $4,850 workshop. They are an audience assembled by a charity's mission content, not by a procurement need.

⚠ That sentence is the load-bearing claim of this whole section and it has never been checked — head-of-social's H2 finding (§7.1 S6). It is an assertion, not a measurement, and it is checkable in ten minutes inside the Instagram app: follower geography, the share of followers on business accounts, and the top-city split. That check is a first-week task in §4.2 and is not optional. If a material share of the 8,800 turn out to be Australian employers, the conclusion of §4.1 changes and the cadence recommendation should be re-taken. Posting to them more often increases exposure to people who are structurally not on the buying committee. More frequency does not expand the addressable buyer set. A different room does. That is exactly what §2 of this plan is, and it is why social gets half an hour and rooms get two and a half.

Against MFC's own prior position (R1 Part 4; STRATEGY-2026-08-24 §5), which said personal-profile posting is a slow trust-building asset rather than lead generation: the external evidence confirms it, with one qualification. It confirms the LinkedIn company-page reach collapse independently (external 2026 sources converge on the same ~1.6% of followers R1 cited). It qualifies the prior position by adding something R1 never gave — a specific floor. "Treat social as infrastructure" is not operational; "two to three posts a week, and below that reach degrades" is. Nothing found contradicts the prior position.

4.2 · The recommended cadence

Channel Cadence Hours
Instagram — feed Two posts a week, and one of the two is a Reel. Fifty weeks. 0.25 h/wk
Instagram — Stories Three a week, at zero incremental creation. 0.08 h/wk
LinkedIn Fortnightly, the stronger post of the fortnight, reworded. No new creation. 0.04 h/wk
Captioning, scheduling, the ten-minute weekly overhead 0.13 h/wk
0.50 h/wk total

⚑ Recosted from 1.0 to 0.5 after the Instagram inbox moved to channel 3 (§7.1 S4). Thirty minutes is deliverable only under three conditions, which are part of the spec and not caveats: (a) the §4.3 library is written once, in the week-one profile build, so no week begins with a blank page; (b) captions for the fortnight are batched in one sitting, not written twice; (c) the Reel is filmed on a day Hunter is already standing in a room — filming it as a separate errand is not thirty minutes and the cadence fails that week.

⛔ The failure mode this creates, named rather than discovered later. Thirty-one standings over fifty weeks means roughly nineteen weeks a year have no room — and posts 1 and 7 of §4.3, the proof post and the question post, are generated by rooms, as is the Reel. In a no-room week the rule is: run posts 2–6 from the library, mirror the previous Reel's rep to camera at home if it is already scripted, and let the Story count drop to one. Going silent is still not permitted; missing the Reel in a no-room week is.

Two one-off costs, funded outside the weekly hour, in week one:

One-off Time Why it comes before post one
The profile build (§4.2a) 90 min The rooms channel sends ~485 people at this profile. Building the feed before the profile is furnishing a house with no front door
The ten-minute Insights check 10 min Named above. It can falsify §4.1's central claim, and it costs nothing
TikTok and Facebook holding-page tidy (§4.6) 60 min Once, then never again

⚑ Three changes head-of-social made to the first draft, and the reasoning is right

1 · One of the two weekly posts is a Reel — 30–45 seconds, face to camera, filmed in the car park after a room, one take.

⛔ One take, but not unscripted — head-of-compliance's H1 finding (§7.2), and it is the sharpest residual catch in the pass. An earlier version specified the Reel as "one take, unedited", which made it the only public surface in this plan where claims review is explicitly removed. Every other public artefact — the eight posts, the captions, the recorded rep, the highlight — is pre-specified copy lifted from claim-checked material. This one was Hunter speaking unscripted, permanent and screenshottable, straight after a room — exactly the emotional state in which an unpermitted sentence gets said. So: the Reel's spoken content is one of §4.3's eight specified shapes, said aloud. §2.5's never-say list applies in full. A take that goes off-script is refilmed, not posted. And no faces without asking — a car park after a room has attendees in it. The first draft contained no video anywhere. On Instagram in 2026 essentially all non-follower distribution runs through Reels, which makes a Reel the only free penetration mechanism the platform offers — and penetration, not intensity, is exactly what §4.1's own structural argument says MFC needs. §4.1's evidence rules out more posting; it does not rule out a different format, and the first draft silently treated those as the same question. Filming on the day of a room means the travel is already paid for. The same file mirrors to TikTok in thirty seconds — a mirror at zero creation cost, not a revival.

⚠ Sourcing limit, stated rather than buried: the Reels-distribution claim is head-of-social's professional judgement, not a sourced 2026 verification — researcher was not run on it. It is strong enough to act on at this cost (fifteen minutes a week) and it should be checked before anyone builds anything larger on it.

2 · Stories at three a week, at zero incremental creation. Stories reach the followers MFC already has, where feed reach is allocated by a ranker. Since §4.1 concludes the existing 8,800 are probably not the buyer set, they should be served by the cheap surface rather than the expensive one. The three: one eight-second Story from every room, same day, filmed on the room from behind (this is the proof post's job done in a tenth of the time, and it can run 36 times a year rather than 12); a re-share of each feed post on publish, ten seconds; and one question sticker a fortnight — "Ask me one thing about mental fitness" — which satisfies the no-self-disclosure rule exactly, because a question is about the world, and which generates post 7 rather than leaving it dependent on a room happening to produce one. A Story reply lands in the same inbox as WEDNESDAY, so it is also capture.

3 · ⛔ The Instagram inbox is channel 3's 0.75 follow-up hours, not social's 0.5. Thirty-six rooms a year driving WEDNESDAY messages into a DM inbox is a reply workload that the social hour would silently absorb and then fail at — and the thing it would fail at is the two-business-day rule, which §1.3 already names as the one thing that is never cut. Ruled explicitly here so it cannot happen by drift. Supporting spec: three saved Quick Replies (WEDNESDAY-received / organiser enquiry / distress-routing) so a human reply takes twenty seconds rather than four minutes.

⛔ And turn off Instagram's default instant-reply. An automated greeting to an incoming DM is precisely the machine-answering-grief failure §4.4 rules against, arriving through a setting nobody chose.

4.2a · The profile is the landing page — spec it before post one

The plan's primary channel ends at this profile. A person photographs the handle in a room on Tuesday and opens it on the couch that night. What they find is the whole of MFC's first impression, and the first draft of this plan specified eight feed posts and none of it.

Element Spec
Bio In organiser and participant language, not category language. The same fix §4.5 prescribes for LinkedIn, applied to the account that actually receives the traffic. It should say what he does — teaches one practical skill for difficult moments, in fifteen minutes, free, for groups — not "building mental fitness."
Three pinned posts, fixed Proof-of-room · one rep · the open invitation. Someone who met Hunter on Tuesday sees all three above the fold.
A Story Highlight titled THE REP ⚑ (named WEDNESDAY in an earlier version, which collided with the DM keyword — a person told one name and shown the other does not find it, which defeats the artefact. §7.2) Five frames, containing the fifteen-minute rep from §2.1. ⚑ This is what the DM'd word is for. Without it, a person sends WEDNESDAY and waits up to two business days for a human to send them something. With it, they get the thing in four seconds and the human reply becomes a conversation rather than a delivery.
⛔ Crisis-line wording, and the line that MFC does not monitor DMs or comments for distress This is a compliance gap in the first draft, not a preference. §4.6 required this wording on the four inactive accounts and never stated it for Instagram — the one account this plan deliberately drives ~485 people toward. Corrected here.

Why two and not the three the evidence's floor allows. The evidence supports 2–3 as the floor and gives no reason at all to go above it for leads. Two is inside the floor at the bottom, and the difference between two and three is roughly twenty minutes a week — which, in a five-hour budget, is a third of the sourcing hour that produces next month's rooms. The plan spends the marginal twenty minutes on the channel with evidence behind it. If a week has slack, the third post is the first thing to add; if a week is tight, one post is the floor and going silent is not permitted.

How two posts a week fits in forty minutes: nothing is created from scratch. §4.3 is a content library drawn from material that already exists and has already been through claims review.

4.3 · S2 — a month of Instagram posts, specified by purpose

The library nobody has used: the workshop's own eleven parts. 02-MERGED-DESIGN P1–P11 is finished, argued, claim-checked material that currently exists only inside a product nobody has bought yet. Every post below is a part of the workshop, lifted. That is what makes an hour a week possible.

The content rule that governs all of them (STRATEGY-2026-08-24 §5, and it is right): every post must be answerable without self-disclosure. A manager will not publicly comment "I'm struggling" — the professional cost is real, permanent and attached to their name. Engagement has to say something about the world, not about the self. Never post anything whose natural reply is a confession, anything asking "how are you really going?", or anything that makes the commenter look like they need help.

# Week Purpose — what this post is for Source part The shape
1 1 Proof MFC is real and was in a room this week. The single most important post type, because it is the only one that answers the question someone asks after meeting Hunter: is this an actual thing? — (the room itself) A photo of a room from behind, or nothing but text. "Fifteen minutes with [gathering] on Tuesday. One thing, taught, done in the room." No faces without asking. No claim about how it went.
2 1 Teach one rep — the copyable unit. The post a person can do while watching. P7 The unit, verbatim in P10(c) and no other wording: "A rep counts when you attempt it, aimed at something that matters, whether or not it works." Plus the stall image. ⚑ This was the third of the three places carrying the banned inversion, and it survived the first correction pass — caught on a self-check, not by a head.
3 2 Name the universal — recognition without diagnosis. The highest-reach shape and the safest. P5 scenario one "Twenty to four on a Wednesday. A message lands. The words are fine. The tone isn't. You read it twice." Nothing else. No lesson attached.
4 2 ⚑ The forwardable. A post whose natural action is not to like it but to send it to one specific person you thought of while reading. This is the only post type written against the metric MFC actually cares about (§3.2: sends). P5 / P7, in a second life-domain The driveway: "Sat in the car for ten minutes last night. Not going in yet. Nothing was wrong — I could just feel I was going to be short with everyone the second I opened the door." Then the rep in three lines. No lesson, no ask, no engagement bait. The person who reads it thinks of someone who does exactly this, and sends it
5 3 The category distinction — the one idea MFC most needs people to hold. P3a The definition, ninety seconds compressed to four lines. "We define it this way: mental health is how you're doing. Mental fitness is what you can actually do."
6 3 Legitimate disagreement — differentiation done honestly, and it generates replies because agreeing costs nothing. P6 "Nobody has ever calmed down because someone told them to. If your fix needs a quiet room and ten minutes, it will never happen at twenty to four on a Wednesday."
7 4 Answer a real question from a room. Turns the rooms channel into content and shows MFC is being asked things. — (from the week's rooms) "Someone asked me on Saturday: isn't this just resilience training? Here's what I said." Then the prepared answer from §2.5.
8 4 The open invitation — the E4 sentence, said publicly, so an organiser can self-select. This is the only post with an ask in it. §2.4 "If you run a group that meets regularly — a run club, a chamber, a shed, a team — I'll come and teach your people one practical thing in fifteen minutes. Free. I'm not selling anything to your members."

The rotation, monthly, indefinitely: proof · teach · universal · forwardable · definition · disagreement · question · invitation.

⚑ Post 4 was originally "state a limit honestly" — we've never delivered a measured cohort — and head-of-marketing cut it (§7.1 M4). The reasoning is right and is worth keeping visible: that sentence is essential in a room and in a buyer conversation, where it is answering a question someone has actually asked. On a public grid, unprompted, it is a charity telling strangers it has no evidence, in a format with no room for the reply. It stays in §2.5's prepared answers and in every buyer conversation; it comes off the content calendar. P19 is not weakened — it is placed where a question precedes it. Post 1 and post 7 are generated by the rooms, which means the two channels feed each other and neither requires invention. Posts 2–6 come from a library that will not run out for two years — eleven parts, each of which yields three or four posts.

What is never posted: anything asking how someone is going; anything whose natural reply is a confession; any statistic; any outcome claim; anyone's face without their asking; any content about a participant's moment, ever. §2.5's never-say list applies identically here, and applies harder, because a post is permanent and screenshottable.

4.4 · S3 — comment-to-reply capture: ruled

Ruling: do not build it. Not now, and not at this volume.

The mechanism was designed in STRATEGY-2026-08-24 §5 and never built. It should stay unbuilt, and the reason is arithmetic, not caution: this plan produces roughly fifteen inbound contacts a month. An automation is engineering, a monthly subscription, a keyword taxonomy and a distress blocklist, in exchange for saving a person perhaps forty minutes a month — at a volume where a human reply is both feasible and materially better than a machine one.

And the risk is asymmetric and specific. The failure the original design named is real: someone comments "I really needed this — I've been struggling since my brother died," and a machine replies with a link. A mental-health charity answering grief with a marketing message, in public, screenshottable. There is a quieter version that is arguably worse — the automation works perfectly, and the person believes MFC saw their distress and responded, when no human saw it at all.

What replaces it: a human, within two business days (§2.3). That is the same promise, delivered better, at seventeen a month.

The requirements, recorded so this is a decision and not an omission — if inbound ever exceeds roughly eighty messages a month, which is the point at which a human reply stops being feasible in the follow-up hour, the mechanism gets built to this specification and no looser:

  1. Keyword-gated trigger only — fires on an exact word the person was explicitly asked to comment. Never on any comment. Never on sentiment.
  2. A distress-language blocklist that suppresses the automation and routes to a human queue instead.
  3. The message discloses that it is automated and that a person reads replies.
  4. One message that delivers the thing, then stops. No sequence.
  5. A named human checking comments daily.
  6. The profile states that MFC does not monitor comments or DMs for distress and cannot respond to a crisis — required whether or not the automation is ever built, and already required by §2.3.
  7. No automation on LinkedIn. Their user agreement prohibits it and they enforce it against accounts.
  8. A privacy determination in place before any list is built from it.

4.5 · S4 — LinkedIn at a few hundred: ruled

Honestly: it does almost nothing yet, and that is the correct expectation for this year.

A few hundred connections, in a market where organic reach fell roughly 50% platform-wide in 2026 and company pages now reach 1.6–5% of their own followers, is not a channel. It has exactly one job, and it is not reach: it is the place a professional checks whether Hunter is a real person before replying to his email about a speaking slot. Rooms 5 through 16 in §2.2 are professional rooms; the organiser who receives the ask in §2.4 will look him up, and LinkedIn is where they will look.

So it gets one fortnightly post, repurposed at no additional creation cost, and is judged on one thing: does the profile make a chamber secretary comfortable saying yes. It is not judged on impressions, followers or engagement, and none of those appear on the monthly page.

One thing to fix, once, in twenty minutes: the profile headline and About section should say what he does in the words a professional organiser uses — not "founder, mental fitness." That is head-of-marketing work of the smallest possible kind and it is worth more than a quarter's posting.

⚑ One ambiguity to resolve, not guessed at. Hunter said "a few hundred on LinkedIn." MFC has a company page (linkedin.com/company/mentalfitnesscollective) and Hunter has a personal profile. The evidence is unambiguous that a company page is close to worthless and a personal profile is not. This plan assumes he means the personal profile and treats the company page as an inactive account under §4.6. If he meant the company page, the ruling below applies to it instead — see FOUNDER QUESTION 1.

4.6 · S5 — the inactive accounts: ruled

Four accounts are linked from the live website, so all four are publicly reachable whether or not anyone is posting to them.

Account Ruling Why
TikTok @mentalfitness.collective HOLD — do not close, do not revive. Closing frees no time and forfeits a handle that matches every other account. Reviving costs a format MFC has no capacity to make well. Post the profile crisis-line wording and one pinned post pointing to Instagram, then leave it. Thirty minutes, once. ⚑ It is also the only account where an ambassador's content model would natively work — if the ambassador programme produces reps on video, this is where they would live, and that is a reason to keep the handle.
Facebook page HOLD, with one job. Do not post. But older community rooms — the Men's Sheds, Rotary, a regional chamber — will check Facebook, not Instagram. So it needs the same treatment as TikTok: correct description, crisis wording, a link, and nothing else.
LinkedIn company page HOLD as an inactive shopfront. Company-page organic reach is 1.6–5% of followers; posting to it is the clearest example in this document of activity that will feel like work and produce nothing. Keep it accurate so it is not embarrassing; never spend an hour on it.
X / Twitter, YouTube, Threads, and any other account CLOSE or leave dormant with no link. ⚑ This plan could not establish which of these MFC actually holds. Only Instagram, Facebook, TikTok and the LinkedIn company page are linked from the live site. Any account that exists, is not one of those four, and has no content, should be closed — an abandoned account with a charity's name on it is a liability, not an asset. Hunter is the only person who knows the full list — FOUNDER QUESTION 2.

The one thing all inactive accounts get, and it is not optional: the crisis-line wording and the line stating MFC does not monitor DMs or comments for distress. An account that receives messages nobody reads is the actual risk here — not the wasted reach.

5.1 · C1 — three options, costed against five hours and one facilitator

A · Virtual, fortnightly (the existing design) B · In person, recurring C · None this year

A recurring physical gathering MFC convenes and owns — the shape Hunter has actually run before. MFC convenes nothing. The rooms in §2 are the front door; §5.3 says what fills the gap.
