Higher per event and better on connection — NOTA reached 250+ at one event — but geographically bounded to one city — ⚑ and on the corrected geography, to the wrong half of it: NOTA and the Bronte Pilates relationship are both Eastern Suburbs (§5.3) ~485 people via §2, which is more than either — and, with Option D, both
What it captures Registration fields at sign-up. The cleanest capture of the three — an email with express consent — but it is the one that needs the privacy determination MFC does not have. A room of names with no mechanism unless the same §2.3 message flow is used Whatever §2.3 captures, which is less structured but needs no determination
Blockers before it can run Six pre-session gates, none discharged: broker confirmation for free public online sessions; a clinician-approved facilitator distress-response card; crisis-line wording on every surface; chat host-only/no breakouts/no recording; privacy policy, collection notice and unbundled consent; the form measuring knowledge not mental state. Plus D-0111 §3 — not authorised for build. All six, plus venue, plus a public-liability position for a recurring public event None
Kill criterion Six sessions with zero paid conversations → it is mission activity, funded as such, not lead generation Attendance below 15 across three consecutive events n/a — reviewed at the six-month whole-plan review

⚑ Option D — co-hosted, quarterly, no MFC-owned cadence. The option that was never screened.

head-of-community's finding, and it is the sharpest single correction in this pass (§7.1). Debra Fidler's AG-069 — co-host one or two free webinars with small, socially respected organisations — is the most relevant advisor position on this question and appeared nowhere in the first draft's reasoning.

D · Co-hosted quarterly
What it is Four sessions a year, each co-hosted with a partner organisation, on their list. MFC owns the content, the facilitation and the data; the partner owns the invitation and opens the session for two minutes
Cost ~3 h each = 12 hours a year. 2.6% of the budget. Against 30h, 40h and 0
Why it dissolves the objection the recommendation leaned on The first draft leaned on "MFC has no list to invite" — wrong twice over, since the MFM cohort exists and Co-hosting borrows someone else's — which is exactly what Debra proposed and exactly why she proposed it
What it produces that the room channel structurally cannot Registration with express consent (AG-066, AG-067) — the one capture shape §2.3's mechanism cannot generate
Cadence risk ⚑ Quarterly, and someone else owns the calendar. No fortnightly promise to decay
Blockers The same six pre-session gates, plus D-0111 §3. All of them, unchanged

There was a twelve-hour option on the table and the first draft costed three options at 52h, 54h and 0. That is the kind of omission that makes a recommendation look decided in advance.

5.2 · C2 — NOTA and the weekly events, weighed as evidence

Two separate pieces of Hunter's own history, and the plan needs both — they say different things. (The definition of done compresses them into one sentence; separating them is what makes the recommendation follow.)

The weekly community events — the decline is the evidence. Hunter's own numbers: 80 → 60 → 50 → 40 → 30 → held at 20–30 for about a year → 15 → 10 → 7 → some nights 2. His own diagnosis, unsparing: "I wasn't able to create a community or an environment that people wanted to return to... What changed is I was trying to solve for a different problem."

NOTA — capacity is the evidence. A separate event brand that pulled 250+ people to a single event, with a real operating team of three or four plus around eight MFC volunteers, collaborations with other communities that worked well. It stopped because Hunter could not run a charity and run NOTA simultaneously. Not because it failed.

⛔ And here the first draft did something it should not have. It quoted Hunter against himself.

It cited, as the load-bearing evidence for killing the community, Hunter at 45:18 on 24 August: "which requires a founder, myself again, to go and set up a whole other community, which takes so much time. Like I can't do that." — presented as his ruling on "a run club or equivalent."

The source record's very next line is: "The recurring session is what he can do." He said that sentence to explain why he chose the recurring session over a run club. The word "equivalent" was doing all the smuggling. Quoted whole, the strongest-looking evidence in this section says the opposite of what the first draft used it for — and Hunter would have spotted it, because he said it.

What the quote actually supports: he will not found and convene a new physical community. It does not support killing a recurring session, and it is not cited for that any more.

Does in-person survive contact with that? No. Option B asks the founder to do the specific thing he ran successfully, at scale, with a team of twelve — and stopped, for capacity reasons that have not improved: MFC now has more surface area, not less, and the team is smaller, not bigger. The evidence against B is not that it does not work. It is that it worked and he could not sustain it, and nothing about 2026 makes that easier.

Does virtual survive it? Partly, and less well than it looks. The decline evidence is about a recurring commitment run by one person whose attention moved — and that is a property of the recurrence, not of the venue. A fortnightly Zoom is a 26-times-a-year standing obligation that must be honoured on the weeks Hunter is delivering a workshop, sick, or fundraising. A cadence announced before it has been held is a promise made by one person on behalf of a calendar that has never been tested — MFC's own strategy said that a year ago and it is still the sharpest sentence written about this.

But the other half of NOTA is a real finding that this plan uses. "Get people in the room first, explain the category second" — and MFC does not have to convene the room to be in it. Collaborating with communities that already exist is the thing NOTA showed worked and is exactly what §2 is. The lesson of NOTA is not "build a community." It is "go to one."

5.3 · C3 — the recommendation, and what replaces the front door

Recommendation: Option D — co-hosted, quarterly, twelve hours a year. Not Option A, and not "none".

⚑ This reverses the first draft's recommendation, and the reason is that head-of-community showed the first draft reached "none" by a route that does not hold: a costing that double-counted the follow-up hour, a founder quote cut in half that said the opposite when quoted whole, and a displacement trade against five professional rooms that the plan had not shown it could get. Right instinct, wrong proof — and Hunter reads the proof.

What survives from the first draft's reasoning, because it is the sound half:

  1. ⛔ The fortnightly MFC-owned session (Option A) is still declined. §5.2's decay reasoning holds for a cadence MFC owns and must honour 26 times a year on the weeks Hunter is delivering a workshop, sick, or fundraising. A cadence announced before it has been held is a promise made by one person on behalf of a calendar that has never been tested.
  2. ⛔ Option B, in-person recurring, is still declined — on capacity, on the decline evidence, and because MFC now has more surface area and a smaller team than when NOTA stopped. ⚑ And on the corrected geography it is worse than the decline evidence alone suggests. NOTA's 250-person event and the Bronte Pilates relationship are both Eastern Suburbs. Option B is therefore not reviving something Hunter has run — it is founding a physical community from zero, in the half of Sydney where MFC has no prior audience, with a smaller team than the one that ran the last.

What changed, and why Option D is the answer:

  1. The recurrence objection proved too much. §5.2's argument was that a recurring commitment decays when the founder gets busy — but channel 1 is a room most weeks for 46 weeks, channel 4 is two posts a week for 50, and channel 5 is monthly. If recurrence-decay were fatal it would kill three of the four surviving channels. It was applied to one option only. The productive version of the argument: recurrence is cheap when someone else owns the calendar — which is Option D, and which is also why §2.2 now targets standing monthly slots in rooms MFC does not convene.
  2. Twelve hours is not 30 or 40. At 2.6% of the budget the displacement argument that carried the whole first draft simply does not arise.
  3. It produces the one thing the rooms cannot: registration with express consent.

⚑ And the "no list to invite" premise was factually wrong. The Mental Fitness Month cohort — 350+ signups, 30–40 ambassadors — exists (§3.5), subject to its consent and currency check.

Still true, and still binding: D-0111 §3 stands — not authorised for build — and the six pre-session gates are undischarged. Option D is a recommendation to Hunter, not an authorisation, and it is FOUNDER QUESTION 4.

What replaces the front door in the meantime — because Option D is four sessions a year, not a front door:

What replaces it as the place people who have heard of MFC actually meet MFC:

The front door What it is
The room MFC walks into ~19 standings a year plus a weekly presence, ~485 people, each of whom meets MFC by doing a rep rather than by hearing about one. This is a better front door than a webinar and it is already funded.
The reply, from a human, within two business days §2.3. The thing a community was going to provide — a person to talk to — provided one-to-one, at the moment of highest interest, at a cost of 0.75 h/wk
The Instagram grid as the place to check MFC is real §4. Someone who met Hunter in a room will look him up that night. What they find is the community's actual job, and it costs an hour a week rather than 23% of the budget
The ambassador's audience Reach, not a room

Held, not discarded — the one thing worth doing at near-zero cost. Hunter's 31 August direction added pre-recorded sessions alongside the live one. The pre-recorded half survives this recommendation and the live half does not: a single recorded fifteen-minute rep, made once from the §2.1 run sheet, costs about three hours total and creates no recurring obligation, no cadence promise and no attendance number to decay. It is the answer to "can you send me that thing you did?" — which is the question the rooms will actually generate. It is not a community and is not counted as one. Its one genuinely open question is where it is hosted, since the site has no page for it — answered below by posting it on the channels MFC already has. (An earlier version also listed the distress-response wording and email consent as unresolved and then resolved both four lines later by assertion, which read as an undischarged gate. They are resolved: crisis wording on screen, and no email is captured, so no privacy determination arises.) ⚑ Corrected on head-of-movement's finding (§7.1 MV5). The first draft made it an unlisted video whose link Hunter sends by hand — a privacy answer to a question privacy did not ask. No email is captured either way. Unlisted-and-sent-by-hand takes the one artefact in this plan that works while Hunter sleeps and makes it unforwardable.

Post it publicly — the Instagram grid and the TikTok handle §4.6 is already holding open for exactly this format. Same three hours, same crisis wording on screen, no page required, no determination required. Held privately it is a leave-behind for people who already met Hunter; held publicly it is the thing a room attendee sends to a colleague, an organiser watches before saying yes (the 90-second cut, §2.4), and an ambassador can send to 180,000 people.

⚑ The trigger is now a dated decision, not a threshold. head-of-community's finding: 60 contacts is not 60 leaders — on the plan's own rates, 60 room contacts scattered across twenty unrelated rooms yields perhaps fifteen people who look after anyone at work, which is a five-person Zoom, precisely the failure §5 predicts. And a threshold has no owner and no date; nobody's job is to check it, and the six-month review arrives first. Replaced with:

A dated decision at month four. Default: no. Flipped only if BOTH hold — 25 or more contacts who answered yes to "do you look after other people at work?", AND one named co-host willing to bring their list. A date forces a decision; a threshold quietly expires.

⚑ And the co-host condition carries a qualifier, added 2026-09-05, because without it the gate is passable by evidence that does not mean what the gate was written to test: the list must be of people who look after others at work. AG-066 and AG-067 are about whose form you get, not about co-hosting as such — so a small, respected community organisation with a list of volunteers and neighbours satisfies the words and defeats the purpose. On this plan's own room list the two candidates that pass both halves are CCWT / ACWA — a training body whose list is supervisors and managers in community services — and AHRI's health, safety and wellbeing network. Both are already channel-2 approaches in the first month, so the co-host question is answered by work the plan is doing anyway.

⛔ Addison Road Community Organisation is specifically not one of them, and the plan pointed at it the wrong way round until head-of-community checked. It is genuinely small and respected and it puts external speakers on its own stage — but addiroad.org.au/workplace-volunteering/ offers paid physical volunteering (hamper packing, landscaping, cooking), not talks or training, and addiroad.org.au/partnerships/ runs sponsorship tiers from $2,500 to $25,000 a year with MFC as the payer. It publishes no webinars at all. A referral route that starts with MFC writing a cheque is not a referral route. Addi Road stays as a possible in-person venue relationship and is not a co-host candidate.

(The first draft's threshold, retained for the record: when the contact list reaches 60 people who have met MFC in a room and replied — ⚑ not, as the first draft wrongly said, "an audience that does not exist today" — the Mental Fitness Month cohort exists (§3.5) — a live session has an audience to invite and a reason to exist. Not before. On this plan's arithmetic that is roughly month five. — superseded by the dated decision above.) At that point the six gates and the privacy determination must also be closed, and the decision is Hunter's, because building it reverses D-0111 §3.

⚑ This differs from Hunter's position of 31 August, which was that the community is fortnightly with pre-recorded sessions added. Noted once, as §2a requires: D-0127 of 5 September explicitly reopened the community as "an option to be evaluated, not the engine," which is the authority this evaluation runs under. If Hunter still wants it built, that is his call and it is condition 9.2 of the definition of done. What the plan asks is that it is built instead of five professional rooms, knowingly, and not on top of five hours that are already spent.

6 · TRACKING (T)

6.1 · T1 — one place every contact lands

The tool: one Google Sheet, named MFC — contacts, owned by Hunter, shared with nobody.

Not a CRM. Not Monday.com. At ~15 contacts a month, a CRM is a monthly cost, a login, an integration and a migration, in exchange for automations MFC has no volume for. The failure mode this plan has to prevent is contacts in five places, not contacts in an unsophisticated place. One sheet, one tab, one row per person, filled in the same 0.75-hour block every week.

Field Why it exists Justification for holding it (AG-016)
date_met The room he stood in Operational
⚑ date_messaged When their message actually arrived ⛔ Without this the median-reply-time number cannot be computed at all — and §1.4 makes it a kill criterion for the whole follow-up channel
⚑ date_replied When a human replied The other half of the same number
⚑ booked_date When they booked stage holds one current value, not a history — so "bookings this month" was not computable
⚑ channel (enum: prof-room / community-room / partner / co-host / social+ambassador) From the keyword they used (§2.3) Countable attribution, which free-text how_found is not. ⚑ co-host added (§7.1): Option D is the recommended community option and was untrackable — no enum value, no line on the monthly page, no source for the month-four gate. social and ambassador are one value because NOTICE cannot separate them (§6.2)
first_name To reply to them like a person Minimum necessary to correspond
contact (their handle or number, as they gave it) To reply They sent it to MFC. Not collected, received
room (which gathering, by name) Tells MFC which rooms produce contacts — the input to the G3 kill criterion The whole channel decision depends on it
room_type (professional / community) The single most important field in the sheet. Without it the two funnels in §1.1 collapse into one and the plan cannot be found wrong Directly drives the arithmetic
leads_people (yes / no / unknown) Their answer to the one question in the reply. Distinguishes a door from a person Determines whether a conversation is even appropriate
how_found (free text) The only attribution MFC has, since there is no link tracking Names which channel worked
stage (contacted / replied / scoped conversation / proposal / booked / no) The funnel, and the monthly page Measures the plan
last_touch (date) Stops people being contacted twice or forgotten Respectful, and operationally necessary
notes (one line, factual) What they actually said ⚑ "—" was a literal AG-016 failure: the one field most likely to capture something sensitive was the only field with no justification written. Now: "One factual line about MFC's own dealings with this person — what they asked for, what was sent, what they said about their organisation. Never anything about how they are."
⛔ Not collected No employer unless they volunteer it; no role; no anything about their mental state, ever; no notes about a person's difficulties. A person who told Hunter something hard in a car park is not a data subject. Nothing about anyone's wellbeing goes in this sheet, in any field, at any time.

⚑ Tab 2 — the room log. No personal data at all.

Eight of the fourteen lines on the monthly page had no source field in the first draft — encounters, rooms stood in, revenue, hours, and the median reply time that a kill criterion depends on. head-of-digital traced every line. The fix is a second tab, and it holds nothing about any person:

date · room · room_type · attendance_estimate · slot_minutes · contacts_from_room · ⚑ largest_org_staff_estimate

⚑ The last field is added 2026-09-05 by J3, and it is the cheapest check in this plan. The professional-rooms channel is credited with 1.0 client on an X2 of 10%, and that rate assumes the room contains someone who could buy a Foundations engagement. The primary segment is purpose-led employers of 20–200 staff; a chamber breakfast and a Rotary dinner are dominated by owner-operators and micro-business. Nothing in the vault verifies which. One estimated number per visit — roughly how many staff the largest organisation in the room employs — and after two visits this stops being an assumption. It records no person and no organisation name, so it changes nothing about privacy. If the answer comes back consistently under thirty, channel 1's 1.0 client is wrong and the hours should move to partnerships.

This is where encounters, rooms-stood-in, weeks-since-a-room and the real replacements for X1, X6 and X7 actually live. It also fixes a subtler defect: counting distinct room values in Tab 1 counts only rooms that produced at least one contact — so a room that produced zero, which is exactly the signal the G3 kill criterion needs, was invisible.

Cost: 30 minutes to build, ~2 minutes a week to fill the room row, out of the existing follow-up block.

⚑ head-of-digital re-traced the page after this fix and found six lines still unsourced — and that the fix had added a sentence claiming every line had a source, which made the page overclaim in a way the first draft did not (§7.2). A false completeness claim on the page Hunter decides from is worse than a missing line. The six: revenue invoiced, hours spent, client organisations won (the plan's entire unit of sale — Tab 1 rows are people, and §6.1 forbids recording an employer unless volunteered), cohorts booked, conversations this month, and the partnership counts. Six further Tab 1 fields and an hours column on Tab 2 close five of them, listed in the template below; the sixth, partnerships, correctly lives in the Monday pipeline and the source note now says so.

⚠ And one residual: NOTICE cannot separate social from ambassador, which the by-channel block reports as 0.5 and 0.2. Either a fourth keyword, or report them as one combined line at 0.7. Until one is chosen, that split falls back to free-text how_found, which §6.1 says is not countable. The plan chooses the combined line — a fourth word asked of an ambassador contradicts §3.3's no-CTA rule.

⚑ And one distinction that must be written down or someone will get it backwards (CLAUDE.md §9e). The contact list never goes to Monday.com — a sheet owned by Hunter and shared with nobody is a tighter privacy posture, with no determination in place, than a shared workspace. The room pipeline — the thirty rooms, their outreach state, slot dates, no personal data — is exactly Monday-shaped and is the project artifact §9e means. That goes to Monday; the contacts do not.

The privacy position, stated once. MFC has no privacy determination. The sheet holds a name and a contact address a person voluntarily sent to MFC, plus operational fields about MFC's own activity. There is a documented justification for every field above (AG-016). Nothing is collected that a person did not hand over, no health information is recorded, and no third party ever receives the list — including a co-host, a partner or an ambassador. When the privacy determination is made, this sheet is the thing it is applied to, and it is small enough to fix. ⚑ Three corrections from head-of-compliance (§7.1 C5).

  • Retention, which the sheet had none of: "A contact with no response to anything for 24 months is deleted at the next monthly review. Holding a row with no live purpose is the thing AG-016 prohibits."
  • The deletion trigger was meaningless as written. "A person can ask to be removed by replying with anything at all" would, read literally, delete anyone who replies "thanks mate." Replace: "If someone asks to be removed, in any words, the row goes that week and I tell them it's done."
  • ⛔ The rule the plan was actually missing: "If a DM contains anything about a person's distress, health or circumstances, none of it is transcribed anywhere. The thread stays in Instagram. MFC does not keep a copy."

⚠ And one narrowing on the consent claim itself. head-of-compliance confirms the consent property holds and signs off on this proceeding without a determination — it is the lowest-risk capture design available to MFC today. But the first draft asserted "nothing here needs a determination first" as a settled fact, and it is a defensible position rather than a settled one. MFC's turnover is ~$20k so the small-business exemption ordinarily applies — except that an organisation providing a "health service" is an APP entity regardless of turnover, and MFC's own insurance declaration reads "Community Health Centre Operation" / "Mental Health Promotion – Support Network", which is the wrong side of that argument in MFC's own paperwork. MFC's position (P1: non-clinical, educational) is the answer and it probably wins — but it is stated here as MFC's position with its reasoning, not assumed. It is a Bird & Bird question that costs nothing to ask alongside the ambassador clauses already going to them.

What is deliberately not on this page: followers, likes, comments, impressions, reach, newsletter opens, website visits. Activity reported instead of results is the failure this page exists to prevent, and every one of those numbers will be higher than the numbers above and will feel better.

6.3 · ⚑ The website work that costs this plan nothing

A real error in the first draft, found by head-of-digital: it budgeted the website against Hunter's five hours, and the website is not built out of Hunter's five hours. Alysse Curran builds the site. The plan's own §0 calls the website "owned elsewhere" and then reasoned about every fix as if it competed with sourcing rooms. It does not compete with anything in this plan.

The right position is not "build the site before starting." It is: the plan runs without the website, and separately, ~2.5 hours of Alysse's time before room 5 removes the two ways the live site actively subtracts from it.

Page Change Cost Why
/inquiry-services-page Delete the empty Wix Bookings block; replace with the four products, one line each, and a link to /contact-us. Copy already exists at 60-projects/mfc-website/rebuild-v2/pages/30-services.md and needs extending to four products. No price required — publishing prices is a separate call ~1.5h Alysse + ~1h head-of-marketing ⛔ MFC's only services page currently reads "Our Services. Nothing to book right now. Check back soon." That is what an HR manager from a professional room reads on the evening they were going to message
/mental-fitness One sentence: future tense → present ~10 min It says "In 2026, we will have dedicated programs… for workplaces" — in September 2026, four days after the products were finished
/our-impact, /mfc-events, /the-daily-1 Retire the live outcome and mechanism claims — "86% of people felt more equipped…", "3 in 4 participants left our events feeling more connected", "strengthens your prefrontal cortex… down regulates your amygdala" — and the November counter running in present tense. Keep "5000+ attendances from 500 individuals" — that is delivery, not outcome ~half a day Alysse, gated on head-of-compliance ruling each line and head-of-marketing supplying replacements ⛔ This is worse than a compliance problem — it is a credibility problem in exactly the rooms this plan is built on. Hunter says in every room "we've never delivered a measured cohort, we've got no results to show you and we're not going to invent any." A chamber HR person who does the diligence concludes either that he is sandbagging or that the site is lying. Both cost the door. Sequence before room 5; rooms 1–4 are warm and will not diligence him
One ambassador credit page So "your name on the wall" (§3.5) is a credit MFC can actually render ~1h Currently offered and undeliverable

Honest limit: none of this makes the site sell. It stops it saying MFC has nothing, and it stops it contradicting the most credible sentence Hunter says. That is the whole of the claim.

7 · WHAT THE HEADS DID (H)

Eight heads were convened as real agent calls, each given the plan in full plus the sources for its own domain, and each asked Hunter's standing question: what would you obviously do differently? A review that finds a document internally consistent and changes nothing has failed. Forty-three numbered findings came back across the eight heads. Forty-one were applied and four were refused with a reason — the two totals overlap because two of the refusals are recorded as findings and two are not.

⚑ (This paragraph has now been wrong twice. It first said "thirty-eight findings, thirty-four applied", counting the headers rather than the rows — three heads list six findings under a heading claiming five. A correction then produced the impossible "thirty-eight came back, thirty-nine were applied". The true count is forty-three numbered rows. A summary line that nobody recounts is exactly the kind of claim this plan keeps catching elsewhere, and it took an external count to catch it here.)

Four were factual errors in the draft, and three of those were caught against MFC's own records rather than against taste.

7.1 · H2 — the improvement pass, per head

head-of-marketing — 5 findings, 4 applied, 1 refused for spoken use

# Finding Status
M1 The organiser email rewritten with the subject line as the ask — "Fifteen minutes for one of your [breakfasts]?" — plus "I sell to organisations, not to people in a room" replacing "people generally like it" (which is not true yet: MFC has zero delivered rooms), the guest-first offer, and one bracketed format word instead of three options APPLIED §2.4
M2 The run-sheet opening reordered: hook first, disclaimers second. The draft opened on three disclaimers, telling twenty-five people who did not come for this what MFC is not, before telling them what they get APPLIED §2.1
M3 Capture changed to photo-first, and the keyword changed from REP (MFC's internal vocabulary; a gym word in a run club) APPLIED §2.3 — and superseded in part by head-of-product and head-of-digital, who showed the single word had to become three, one per channel. Marketing's reasoning was right and its scope was one channel too narrow
M4 Grid post 4 ("we've never delivered a measured cohort") cut from the public calendar and replaced with a forwardable post. That sentence is essential in a room and in a buyer conversation, where it answers a question someone asked; on a public grid, unprompted, it is a charity telling strangers it has no evidence in a format with no room for the reply APPLIED §4.3
M5 A positioning sentence for professional rooms APPLIED §2.4a — but rewritten by head-of-compliance, whose version is the one in the plan
M6 A rhetorical framing about the number of small moments in a life ⛔ REFUSED FOR SPOKEN USE. §2.5's never-say list bars any statistic, and a rhetorical figure is indistinguishable from a claimed one once it has been said aloud in a room with no leave-behind. Recorded here so the refusal is visible rather than silent
Biggest thing wrong "The plan has no message and no WIIFM for the twenty-five people in the room" — it told the organiser what was in it and never told the room ACCEPTED. M2's reordering is the fix, plus the tell-one-person line at the close

head-of-commercial — 5 findings, all applied. The largest structural change in the pass.

# Finding Status
CM1 Change the unit of sale from 13 bookings to 4 client organisations. 8 · FOUNDER QUESTIONS

Everything else in this plan was decided rather than asked. These are the items where one of the six protected conditions in CLAUDE.md §6a is genuinely met — Hunter's own judgement is the deciding variable, information exists nowhere in the vault, or the decision reverses a standing record. Each arrives with a recommendation, not a bare question.

FQ1 · Which LinkedIn did you mean?

"make the strategy for our instagram having 8800 followers now, and a few hundred on linked In."

MFC has a company page (linkedin.com/company/mentalfitnesscollective) and Hunter has a personal profile. The evidence is unambiguous that a company page reaches 1.6–5% of its own followers and a personal profile reaches roughly 5.6× more. The plan assumes the personal profile (§4.5) and treats the company page as an inactive shopfront (§4.6). Recommendation: confirm it is the personal profile. If the few hundred are on the company page, the honest answer is that the channel is worth even less than §4.5 says, and the twenty-minute profile fix moves to the personal profile instead. Information that exists nowhere in the vault — condition 1.

FQ2 · Which other social accounts exist?

Only four are linked from the live website: Instagram, Facebook, TikTok and the LinkedIn company page. Hunter referred to "socials which I am not active on at the moment", which may include others. Recommendation: close any account MFC holds that is not one of those four and has no content. An abandoned account carrying a mental-health charity's name, receiving messages nobody reads, is a liability rather than a dormant asset. Only Hunter knows the full list — condition 1.

FQ3 · "When No One's Watching" — the question has changed. It is not does this exist; it is Rob, would you introduce us?

⛔ This question was wrong for two drafts and the answer was in the vault the whole time. Both said "R1 searched for it twice and could not identify the entity, and nothing in the vault describes it," and both excluded it from §2.2 on that basis.

10-sources/fathom-transcripts/2026-07-20-committee-strategy-session-business-plan.md, Rob Dooley at 44:06 and 44:19: "Have you heard of the group, what's it called, When No One's Watching?… They go for a swim in the morning, have a cup of coffee. It was started by one of the ex-Swan's footballers that's down at Marooba, and I'm on their WhatsApp group with 1,750 other like-minded."

It is a morning ocean swim and coffee group at Maroubra, founded by a former Sydney Swans player, and a named MFC committee member is on its 1,750-person WhatsApp group. ⛔ That is the largest single audience named anywhere in this plan, it is held open by someone MFC already has, and two web searches concluded it did not exist because nobody read forty lines further into a transcript this vault already held. It never needed verifying. It needed one message.

Recommendation: ask Rob directly, not the committee as a body. ⚠ The geography is Eastern Suburbs and the travel objection in §2.2 applies — but a warm introduction from a committee member to a 1,750-person group is not a cold room and must not be priced as one. Condition 1 is discharged. What remains is a request to a person, which is Hunter's to make.

FQ3a · ⛔ Ice International Cycling — Rob has already paid $30,000 to be in the room MFC is looking for. What is the route in?

⛔ This is the largest single thing this build missed, and it was nine lines past the two quotes the 440 section above is proud of recovering — in the same passage, in reply to the same sentence. Rob Dooley, 20 July, 45:54, answering Hunter's "I'm a cycling club person" directly:

"And the reason I was saying… the executives is there's a thing called Ice International Cycling… where it's an invite-only networking group and… I sponsored one of their breakfasts for $30,000 for a breakfast, right, to access, you know, a group of people for one event, where there's CEOs and leaders, there's money."

It appears nowhere else in this vault. It lands on the exact intersection the plan had just called unfindable: a room type Hunter says he is genuinely in, a demographic with purchasing authority, and a warm route through MFC's own commercial advisor, who has personally paid to be in it.

What was sourced on 2026-09-05, and what was not. The organisation is real and now trades as Cycling Execs — Rob's "Ice International Cycling" is the older name, International Cycling Executives (ICE), and internationalcyclingexecutives.com now serves the new brand.

Fetched 2026-09-05
What it is "a trusted peer group for executives who ride" — application-only, senior executives "typically GM-level and above", run under Chatham House Rules. cyclingexecs.com
Where Hubs at Sydney, Melbourne, Canberra, Singapore. LinkedIn lists the HQ at Crows Nest, NSW, with London and Singapore offices, 5,377 followers, 11–50 staff. au.linkedin.com/company/international-cycling-executives
The format "At the centre of it is the ride. Every event starts on the bike, which means by the time you sit down, the conversation has already begun."
How you get in "Membership is by application. We read every one." — and separately, it requires "a member who knows you and can vouch for you." info@cyclingexecs.com · app.cyclingexecs.com/apply
⚠ Cadence NOT STATED on either page fetched. By this plan's own §2.2 grading that is LISTED, not VERIFIED
⚠ Whether it takes outside speakers Unknown, and deliberately not claimed. A search summary described "thought leadership… presentations and panel discussions" at ICE breakfasts; neither page I actually fetched contains that wording, and the pages that did are gone. It is not printed here as fact — that is the same fetch discipline §2.2 is built on

⛔ Why it is a founder question and not a §2.2 room — three sourced reasons, not a preference

  1. There is no cold route. E2 asks for a route to a slot. Entry requires a member to vouch for you. A form MFC can fill in is not a route into a room that admits by referral. Rob is the route, and he is the only one MFC has.
  2. No cadence is published, so it cannot be graded VERIFIED without breaking the scale §2.2 applies to every other row.
  3. ⛔ MFC cannot buy in, and the $30,000 says so. That figure is sponsorship of one breakfast — the price Rob paid for access for one event. MFC operates on roughly $12,000 in total. Anything that reads this as a purchasable channel has misread it by a factor of three.

And it is not an Inner West room. The HQ is Crows Nest and no Sydney venue is published, so the travel question that governs the 440 governs this too.

The question for Hunter, and it is one sentence to one person

"Rob — you mentioned Ice International Cycling on 20 July. Would you introduce me, and is there any format where I'd give their bunch fifteen minutes?"

Recommendation: ask, and ask Rob directly rather than raising it with the committee as a body. It costs one message. The realistic outcome is not a booking — it is finding out whether the room takes speakers at all, which nothing in the vault or on the web currently answers. ⚠ Carry Hunter's own reservation from the same session (41:57): "It's an-saturated space. Like, I know there's so many running exec, there's so many things." He may well judge this the wrong room, and that is his call to make with the facts in front of him rather than with the item missing.

Condition 6 — this turns on Hunter's relationship with a committee member, which is his to spend.

FQ4 · MFC's own community — the plan recommends Option D, co-hosted quarterly

This is the one genuine reversal in the document, and it is condition 9.2 of the definition of done. §5.3 declines Option A (MFC-owned fortnightly, ~30h) and Option B (in-person recurring, ~40h) — on the founder's own decay history, on displacement, and because a cadence MFC owns must be honoured on the weeks Hunter is delivering a workshop, sick, or fundraising. It does not recommend "none". ⚑ The first draft did, and reached it by a route that does not hold: a costing that double-counted the follow-up hour, a founder quote cut in half that says the opposite when quoted whole (§5.2), and a displacement trade against five professional rooms the plan had not shown it could get.

Option D — four sessions a year, each co-hosted with a small respected organisation, on their list — costs ~12 hours a year (2.6% of the budget). It is AG-069, Debra Fidler's own proposal, and it was never screened in the first draft. It borrows the list MFC does not have, someone else owns the calendar so there is no cadence to decay, and it produces the one thing the rooms structurally cannot: registration with express consent (AG-066, AG-067).

⚑ This differs from his own position of 31 August, which was fortnightly with pre-recorded sessions added. D-0127 of 5 September reopened the community as "an option to be evaluated, not the engine" — which is the authority this evaluation runs under, and the reason the difference is being surfaced once rather than silently resolved either way.

Recommendation: accept Option D, and post the pre-recorded rep publicly — one recorded fifteen-minute rep, ~3 hours once, no cadence promise, no attendance number to decay, on the grid rather than sent by hand (§5.3). The go/no-go on the first co-hosted session is a dated decision at month four: default no, flipped only if BOTH hold — 25 or more contacts who answered yes to "do you look after other people at work?", AND one named co-host willing to bring their list. ⚑ And the co-host's list must be of people who look after others at work — on this plan's own list that is CCWT / ACWA or AHRI, both already first-month partner approaches, and it is not a community organisation whose list is volunteers and neighbours. §5.3 carries the full reasoning and the one candidate that was pointed at the wrong way round.

⛔ Still binding: D-0111 §3 — not authorised for build — and the six pre-session gates and the privacy determination are undischarged. Option D is a recommendation, not an authorisation.

If Hunter wants Option A built instead, that is his call and it is legitimate — but the plan asks that it be built instead of five professional rooms, knowingly, rather than on top of five hours already committed. Conditions 3 and 6, and it reverses a standing decision record.

FQ5 · The social cadence — the evidence came back "no"

Hunter's own condition was "open to it if evidence supports this move/approach." §4.1 answered it honestly and the answer is no: every source that measures posting frequency measures it against reach or engagement, not one measures it against enquiries, and the structural argument (Ehrenberg-Bass: growth comes from reaching more actual category buyers, not from more exposure to an existing follower base) says an 8,800-follower charity audience is not where a $4,850 buyer is. What the evidence does support is a floor: below 2–3 posts a week, reach degrades.

Recommendation: two Instagram posts a week and one LinkedIn, inside one hour, and no more. This is Hunter's to accept because he raised the possibility and the answer disappoints it. The observable that would change it is named in §4.1 — if inbound messages naming a room, a rep or a post exceed four a month at the low cadence, the channel is doing more than the evidence predicts and the case for raising cadence becomes real. Condition 6.

FQ6 · Anything Tom is offered

§3.1 is a recommendation about what MFC can honestly offer. The offer itself is Hunter's, in person, at the coffee. Nothing in this plan should be sent to Tom as a document from MFC's system; the two-page pack in §3.4 is Hunter's to write in his own words. The plan's one substantive input: the honest answer to "is it enough?" is that it is enough for the first term and not obviously enough for a second, and what makes a second term worth it is evidence his reps reached people — which MFC will not have until it measures. Condition 6, and §9.3 of the definition of done.

FQ7 · R1's best-bet route is now IN this plan as a fifth channel — do you accept the 0.9 hours?

R1-demand-generation-research.md ranks WHS consultant, broker and EAP referral partnerships as Route 1 — "the one route I would bet on" — on the reasoning that those consultants sit directly downstream of the strongest live purchase trigger in the market (an improvement notice from one of the 20 new NSW psychosocial inspectors), have the relationship and the deadline, and lack exactly the delivery-and-measurement product MFC has.

The first draft of this plan left it out, on the grounds that D-0127's founder direction named four channels and this was not one of them. head-of-commercial showed that reading was wrong on D-0127's own text: Hunter listed what he intends to do; he did not rule out what MFC should build. And the judgement being silently inherited — that consultants and brokers are "organisational doors, not lead sources" — belongs to D-0116, which D-0127 withdrew with the words "nothing in D-0116 is carried forward by default." The plan was enforcing a withdrawn decision, and D-0127 says the opposite about R1: it "is evidence the plan must weigh."

So it is now in, at 0.9 hours a week, and it is the single largest source of clients in the plan — 2.0 of the 4.0. Ten approaches (Safety Australia Group, iHR Australia, Lockton, three mid-tier EAPs, plus AHRI, CCWT/ACWA, NDS and LGNSW/StateCover relabelled out of the room list), a 4-hour one-off build in month one taken from the flex ceiling, and R1 §8's own falsification test attached.

The question for Hunter is therefore not "should this be carried?" but: D-0127 named four channels and this plan runs five. Do you accept the 0.9 hours a week, taken from social (−0.5) and ambassador (−0.4)? Recommendation: yes. It is the only route where someone else already holds the trigger, the relationship, the deadline and the credibility and is missing exactly what MFC has, and it needs no track record because a partner evaluates MFC's method and measurement, which exist, rather than its client list, which does not. Condition 2 — an authoritative source and a founder direction point different ways, and reconciling that silently is exactly what §4 forbids.

⚠ One thing to re-check before the 4-hour build: R1 is dated 2026-08-10 and its market claims (the 20 new NSW psychosocial inspectors, Safety Australia Group's partnership programme, iHR's positioning, StateCover's incentive programme) were not re-verified for this plan.

FQ8 · The ambassador programme's own status is unresolved in the record

The ambassador programme was parked as reactive-only in June and reactivated by August, and MFC's Theory of Change marks that reversal "not mine to close." §3 of this plan assumes it is live. Recommendation: close it explicitly — one line at the next committee meeting confirming the programme is active — before the Mental Fitness Month cohort is contacted or the pack goes to Tom. It costs two minutes and it is the difference between a programme and an assumption. Condition 2 — authoritative sources materially conflict.

FQ9 · Whose phone number goes on the card?

§2.3's fallback for rooms where Instagram is the wrong channel — a Men's Shed, a room of people over 60 — put Hunter's personal mobile number on a card photographed by strangers, permanently, on an account that will eventually receive a distressed message. Recommendation: a second number used for MFC only (a prepaid SIM or a free VoIP number), or drop the fallback and hand those rooms a card carrying only the Instagram handle. This is founder safety and it is his call — condition 6.

FQ10 · Does MFC hold a current NSW fundraising authority?

head-of-compliance could not verify it in the vault and named it rather than softening it: whether an ambassador with a 180,000 audience soliciting or receiving donations for MFC engages the Charitable Fundraising Act 1991 (NSW) third-party fundraiser provisions. MFC has raised money publicly before, so an authority may exist. If Tom ever says "chip in," this becomes live. Recommendation: "no fundraising asks" stays on the never-say list until the committee confirms the position — which is where it currently sits in §3.3. Condition 4 — a legal boundary requiring human authority.

FQ11 · Five hours produces about ten cohorts. Minimum viable is thirteen.

This is the decision the whole document now turns on, and it is at the top of the plan as well as here. The adversarial pass broke a frame that both head rounds improved inside without weighing: the hours table summed to 5.00 because two cells were plugged, the recommended community option was costed at zero, ~65 hours a year of named work was in no table, and thirteen cohorts was only reached by counting three channels the plan itself disclaims.

Honest position: ~6.2–7.5 hours a week steady state, ~10.9 in month one, ~3.0 client organisations, ~10 cohorts. ⚑ Corrected 2026-09-05 — the previous wording carried ~12, ~3.1 and the pre-correction community figure, all three of which had been fixed elsewhere and not here. L-045 exactly: a targeted check reported as if it were a document check, in the one section Hunter decides from. ⚠ And read the two sensitivities under §1.1's total before acting on the 3.0 — 1.0 of it rests on a rate §2.2 marks unheld, and five of the eight verified professional rooms are Rotary.

Three real forks:

What it means
(a) Accept ~10 cohorts A sub-minimum-viable year one, honestly labelled. MFC does not clear the money case's minimum tier and must cover the gap from elsewhere
(b) Give seven to eight hours The plan reaches ~13. But 15-THE-MONEY-CASE puts delivery at 11.5 hours a week at that volume, so this is a ~19-hour MFC week — and no document in the chain has shown Hunter that total
(c) Change the mix or the price 15-THE-MONEY-CASE §2 is where that argument lives. Not a lead-generation decision
⛔ (d) Reallocate the zero-yield room hours — the fork this plan decided silently and should not have ⚑ Surfaced by the skeptic pass, 2026-09-05, and it is genuinely uncomfortable. §1.1 now assigns X2 = 0% to eight of the nineteen standings — the Men's Sheds, the Probus clubs, the Older Women's Network, the two community centres, the 2pm-weekday men's group — "deliberately and permanently." Those eight standings are 42% of the room channel and are modelled to produce zero doors. Moved to professional standings they are ~200 more professional encounters ≈ +0.7 clients ≈ +2.3 cohorts, taking the plan to ~3.7 clients and roughly twelve cohorts, at five hours and no extra founder time. ⛔ The trade is ~120 people meeting mental fitness, for near-minimum-viable revenue. The plan made that trade in Hunter's favour by making the retiree rooms unkillable-on-leads, and then presented him a shortfall as though the only levers were his hours and his price. It is a mission-against-survival decision, it is §6a condition 6, and it is his

Recommendation: (a) for the first quarter, then decide in December on MFC's own data. Months 1–3 produce no bookings at any conversion rate; what they produce is the first real value of X4, and every fork depends on it. Choosing in December on three months of real numbers beats choosing today on assumptions. Conditions 5 and 6 — this is Hunter's time, MFC's survival, and his call.

Original source: 60-projects/lead-generation-v3/01-THE-PLAN.md

02 Independent Review

Independent review — lead generation v3

Scored against 00-DEFINITION-OF-DONE.md (frozen), condition by condition, on each condition's own pass observable column. Twenty-six conditions. Arithmetic re-derived rather than accepted.

Result: 22 PASS · 3 PARTIAL · 1 FAIL. Verdict: SHIP, with E2 as a blocking pre-execution fix.

1 · THE ARITHMETIC — G

G1 · The conversion chain, stated with numbers — PARTIAL

Evidence. §1.1 gives the assumption table (X1, X2 tiered, X3, X4, X4p, X6/X7), each carrying an explicit ASSUMPTION mark and a basis note, and §1.2 gives a P10/P50/P90 band. The chain re-derives correctly on independent recomputation: channel 1 (1.0 ÷ 0.20 = 5 → ÷ 0.60 = 8.3 → ÷ 0.10 = 83 → ÷ 0.30 = 278 → ÷ 25 = 11.1 standings); channel 1b (20 × 30 = 600 × 15% = 90 × 3% = 2.7 → 0.32 clients); channel 2 (6 scoped × 32% = 1.92); channel 4 (20 ÷ 8 = 2.5 × 20% = 0.5). Total 4.0. §6.2 carries the per-month targets (23/50 encounters, 14 contacts, 1.5 conversations, 0.33 clients, 1.1 cohorts), which supplies the "per month" the condition asks for.

What is missing.

  1. Channel 2's own funnel is unsourced and unmarked. "Ten approaches → ~4 partner conversations → ~3 partners who actually engage → ~2 qualified referrals each" carries no ASSUMPTION tag, no P10/P90 band and no basis. This is the largest single source of clients in the plan (2.0 of 4.0) and it is the one place the condition's own pass observable is not met.
  2. Channel 5's 0.2 clients is asserted, not derived. No inputs at all.
  3. The revenue band is arithmetically linear where it cannot be. $38,000 at 13 cohorts implies ~$2,923/cohort, and P10/P90 are computed as 6 × $2,923 = ~$17,600 and 19 × $2,923 = ~$56,000. But the money case's $38,257 is stated as revenue above every cost including paying Hunter — fixed costs do not scale with cohort count, so the P10 figure overstates. §1.2 half-notices this in its own "×1.4 warning" note and then prints the linear number anyway.
  4. Minor: §6.2's "conversations 18/yr" does not reconcile against the channels' own scoped-conversation counts (5 + 6 + ~0.5 + 2.5 ≈ 14), and "lead people 11/yr" is derived nowhere.

G2 · The five hours allocated, with the flex rule — PASS

§1.3's table: 0.75 + 1.75 + 0.75 + 0.5 + 0.90 + 0.35 = 5.00 exactly. Day-named, readable in a minute. The flex rule is written in both directions with a trigger, a ceiling (8 hours, capped at six weeks a year, tracked) and an order of what gives way (social → ambassador → sourcing, never follow-up). One-off builds are explicitly funded outside the weekly five.

G3 · Kill criterion and review date per channel — PARTIAL

§1.4 gives criteria and dates for professional rooms, partnerships, community rooms, follow-up, social, ambassador and the whole plan, and the rewrite reasoning ("a waived kill criterion is worse than none") is sound.

What is missing: the recommended community option has no kill criterion. §5.1 gives kill criteria for Options A, B and C, and Option D — the one actually recommended — has a "Blockers" row and no kill criterion. §1.4's channel table has no community-session row either. The single option the plan asks Hunter to accept is the one that cannot be stopped by rule.

2 · INTO EXISTING COMMUNITIES — E

E1 · The fifteen minutes, minute by minute — PASS

Minutes checked, both tables.

Main table: 0.75 + 1.5 + 2.25 + 1.0 + 2.5 + 2.25 + 1.75 + 1.5 + 1.5 = 15.00. Contiguous, no gap, no overlap.

Cut order: 15:00 − 0.75 = 14:15 ✓ · −1.0 = 13:15 ✓ · −0.75 = 12:30 ✓ · −0.75 = 11:45 ✓ · −1.0 = 10:45 ✓ · −0.75 = 10:00 ✓ · −0.25 = 9:45 ✓. All seven rows correct. Cut 6 lands the ten-minute version at exactly 10:00, which makes cut 7 a genuine reserve, as claimed.

Five-minute build: 0:08 + 1:30 + 1:45 + 0:30 + 0:30 + 0:40 = 5:03, as stated.

The sheet is deliverable-from-tomorrow, sourced to P3a–P7 line by line, with a never-cut list, a room-type scenario swap and a pause variant. The 12:00 block makes the room do a rep rather than watch one, which is what "one rep, taught, done in the room" requires.

E2 · Fifteen named gatherings with contact route and cadence — FAIL

Counted.

  • Eighteen rooms are named (§2.2) with routes.
  • Rooms 5, 10, 11 and 12 are moved out of the channel to partnerships — explicitly, with the reasoning that they are institutional relationships, not places to stand for fifteen minutes.
  • Rooms 8 (BNI) and 17 (Fishburners) are deleted.

Usable gatherings remaining: 12 — rooms 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 7, 9, 13, 14, 15, 16, 18. Three short of the condition's floor of fifteen.

Three further problems inside the twelve:

  1. Room 9 (Vistage) falls out of the arithmetic entirely. The split line assigns "rooms 6, 7, 13–16" as professional and "2, 3, 4, 18" as community, with room 1 counted once. That is eleven rooms. Room 9 belongs to neither category and is counted in no funnel.
  2. Cadence is missing or unverified on several. Room 3's cadence is "not recorded in the vault, confirm on the call"; room 4 has no cadence at all ("individually and through sites"); rooms 14–16 give "published events calendar" / "recurring breakfasts" rather than a cadence.
  3. The plan itself concedes the community half is a hole (schools, P&C, RSLs — "nothing verifiable came back") and defers it to "one afternoon" of phone calls that has not happened.

This is the exact weakness the spec's §10 flagged and asked node 2 to close. Node 2 named more rooms than node 1 and then removed six of them, ending below the floor. The route work done on the twelve is genuinely good — four are already warm, which is the finding §10 asked for — but the count is not met.

To close it: name three or more additional gatherings that survive the plan's own screen (a community room with a standing slot, not another chamber), with route and cadence, and assign room 9 to a funnel. This is one sourcing hour, not a rebuild.

E3 · What MFC leaves with — PASS

§2.3. Person-initiated inbound message, per-channel keyword (WEDNESDAY / ATTEMPT / NOTICE), photo-first, printed A5 card, THE REP highlight with its tradeoff stated and instrumented, native instant-reply only with a named prohibition on third-party DM tooling. Works with no website, no page, no wifi and no privacy determination — which is the condition's actual test. Three failure points named including the Message-Requests silent failure. The personal-mobile exposure is correctly escalated rather than decided (FQ9).

E4 · What the gathering gets — PASS

§2.4. One repeatable sentence, decomposed into three load-bearing parts against AG-020/AG-026, with the loss side ("he's not selling anything to your members") identified as the part that gets the yes. The full ask email is written with the subject line as the ask, and "people generally like it" is correctly removed as an untrue claim. What is explicitly not offered (data, reports, co-branding) is named so the favour does not become admin.

E5 · Claims safety in a room with no leave-behind — PASS

§2.5. Permitted set enumerated by reference (P1, P2, P4, P10(c), P13, P14, P19, P31, P34, P36), three must-say sentences, a never-say list that specifically bars any statistic and the "3,000 people / 78%" figures by name, and four prepared answers including "that's me." The P10(c) verbatim correction (the banned inversion, caught in three places) and the P37 pairing rewrite are substantive, not assertions. The section correctly states its own limit — K-08's red lines are themselves unapproved.

3 · THE AMBASSADOR PROGRAMME — A

A1 · What is in it for Tom, honestly — PASS

§3.1. Seven items, every one stated in his terms and traceable to something he said or to the programme draft — the answer to his own "who am I to share these things?", naming rights given away before he asks, authorship of an unsolved problem rather than distribution of a finished asset, a structured no-fault exit. The honest verdict is given rather than dodged: enough for the first term, not obviously enough for a second, with the dependency named (evidence his reps reached people, which MFC will not have until it measures). The three-hour floor is the stated protection if the answer is no.

A2 · What MFC gets, measured — PASS

§3.2. Numbers per ambassador per year (one door, one person brought, 2–4 sessions), each with the recording mechanism named (the one sheet with channel = ambassador; the session recap). Sends are explicitly demoted to a diagnostic looked at with Tom and never reported as MFC's number; likes, comments and follower growth are excluded by rule. The resolution of the §3.2/§3.3 contradiction — a lead target that the no-CTA rule made impossible to cause or identify — is correct, and the NOTICE keyword gives the route without asking Tom for a CTA.

A3 · What he can say and never say — PASS

Checked for substance, not assertion. Present in §3.3: the can-say/never-say split with the added never-say lines (no MFC product/evidence claims, no quoting a received DM, no fundraising ask); the three self-tests; the worked programme-ending caption with its six breaches itemised; the incident response (delete, no repost, committee within 48h, no public statement without Eimear and Bird & Bird); four saved DM replies with 13YARN; the rewritten reply-2 close; and the trigger rule.

The trigger rule is the substantive test and it passes it. The first draft told Tom never to assess severity and then required him to choose between two replies, which is an assessment. The rewrite — "Reply 1 is what you send. Always," with reply 2 gated on the person's own stated words — converts a clinical judgement into a keyword match. That is a real compliance call, not a sign-off sentence.

The head-of-compliance verdict is SIGNED OFF WITH CONDITIONS with nine named conditions, each traceable to a specific text change, plus items it explicitly would not license: reply 2's wording (routed to Mindframe), the NSW Charitable Fundraising Act question (FQ10), and the BUP016 / ABUSE EXCLUSION 0923 gap on one-to-one DM contact. A review that names what it cannot sign is the evidence this condition asks for. The protected-title correction ("counsellor" is not protected in Australia) is factually right and is the kind of catch a real call produces.

A4 · The sequence respects what is not agreed — PASS

§3.4. Weeks 1–2, 2–3 and 3–4 each carry an explicit "what does not happen" column reading no post, no mention of Tom outside MFC, no follow-up nudge. Four pre-first-post gates. The ambassador-not- spokesperson framing is reinstated. Nothing public, Tom named nowhere.

A5 · Who else, and why — PASS

§3.5. Two further archetypes with routes: the sub-elite / ex-athlete — with A-22's reversal of Hunter's original idea explicitly respected and its three reasons restated — routed through clubs, not follower counts; and the tradie or shift worker at 900–5,000 followers, routed through the §2.2 trade rooms and sheds, with head-of-community's correction that the original sourcing route was an empty set and must be inverted. Tom does not recruit. The who-looks-right-and-will-not-work list is recorded so it is not re-proposed. The MFM cohort of 350+ signups / 30–40 ambassadors is surfaced as the place to look first, with its consent-and-currency caveat and the live registered conflict routed to FQ8. Three, not five, and costed.

4 · SOCIAL — S

S1 · The evidence question, answered — PASS

§4.1. Seven findings for and against, each tiered by source quality (sample-size evidence, vendor claim, N=1 weak, marketing-science), including a stated NOT FOUND with the search terms recorded. The structural argument (Ehrenberg-Bass penetration vs intensity) is what decides it, and the answer is the unflattering one: no, more posting does not produce leads here — but there is a floor at 2–3 posts a week. The load-bearing claim ("the 8,800 are not the buyers") is correctly flagged as an assertion, not a measurement, with a ten-minute in-app check placed as a first-week task that can falsify the section. What Hunter would have to see to go higher is named (FQ5: >4 inbound a month naming a room, a rep or a post).

S2 · A month of Instagram posts, by purpose — PASS

§4.3. Eight posts, each with a purpose column (proof · teach · universal · forwardable · definition · disagreement · question · invitation), a source part from the workshop's own P1–P11, and a shape. The content rule (STRATEGY-2026-08-24 §5 — answerable without self-disclosure) governs all of them and is applied, not cited. The eleven-part library is used as the condition intended. Posts 1 and 7 are generated by rooms, and the no-room-week failure mode is named with a rule rather than discovered later.

S3 · Capture from social, ruled — PASS

§4.4. Ruling: do not build it, on arithmetic (~15 contacts a month) rather than caution, plus the asymmetric-risk argument including the quieter failure (the automation works and the person believes a human saw them). Requirements are recorded as an eight-item specification with a stated trigger volume (~80 messages a month), which is what makes it a decision rather than an omission.

S4 · LinkedIn, ruled — PASS

§4.5. Honest and short: it does almost nothing yet, it has exactly one job (the place a professional checks Hunter is real before answering the speaking-slot email), it is judged on that one thing and on no vanity metric, it gets one fortnightly repurposed post and a twenty-minute headline fix. The company-page vs personal-profile ambiguity is surfaced rather than guessed (FQ1).

S5 · The inactive accounts, ruled — PASS

§4.6. TikTok HOLD · Facebook HOLD with one job · LinkedIn company page HOLD as shopfront · everything else CLOSE or dormant-with-no-link. The reason each ruling is what it is, is given. The set that cannot be enumerated from the vault is escalated (FQ2) rather than ignored. The crisis-line and no-DM-monitoring wording is required on all of them, which is the actual risk on a dormant account.

5 · MFC'S OWN COMMUNITY — C

C1 · Three options costed — PASS

§5.1. Options A, B and C are each costed against five hours and one facilitator, with hours per occurrence, occurrences a year, annual cost as a share of budget, what it displaces, reach, what it captures, blockers and a kill criterion. The recosting of A from 52h to ~30h is shown with its reason (a double-counted follow-up hour already funded in channel 3). A fourth option (D, co-hosted quarterly, 12h) is added and a recommendation is given.

(Option D's own missing kill criterion is scored under G3, not here — the condition asks for three options and gets them.)

C2 · NOTA weighed as evidence — PASS

§5.2. Both halves of the history are separated and used differently: the weekly decline (80 → 2, with Hunter's own diagnosis) and NOTA's 250+ capacity event that stopped for capacity, not failure. The answer to "does in-person survive contact with it" is given directly — no — with the reason that the founder ran exactly this successfully with a team of twelve and stopped, and the team is now smaller.

The strongest thing in this section is a self-caught integrity defect. The first draft cited Hunter's "Like I can't do that" as his ruling against a recurring community; the source record's very next line is "The recurring session is what he can do." The plan surfaces the misquote, states what the quote actually supports, and stops using it for the killed conclusion. That is the condition's "cites it" done properly.

C3 · If recommended: how people are reached and what is tracked — PARTIAL

How people are reached is answered — the co-host's list and invitation, which is the whole point of Option D — and what replaces the front door is written either way (§5.3's four-row table: the room, the human reply within two business days, the grid, the ambassador's audience), plus the publicly posted recorded rep.

What is missing is the tracking half, for the option actually recommended. "Registration with express consent" names the capture shape but not the instrument. Concretely:

  • §6.1's channel enum is prof-room / community-room / partner / social / ambassador — there is no value for a co-hosted session, so an Option D registrant cannot be attributed in the one place every contact is meant to land (T1).
  • §6.2's monthly page has no line for co-hosted sessions — not in encounters, not in the by-channel block.
  • The month-four dated decision is a good replacement for the abandoned 60-contact threshold, but one of its two gates ("25 or more contacts who answered yes to do you look after other people at work?") depends on the leads_people field, which is captured — and the other ("one named co-host willing to bring their list") has no owner, no field and no place on the monthly page.

To close it: add a co-host channel value, one monthly-page line, and a named owner for the co-host gate.

6 · TRACKING — T

T1 · One place every contact lands — PASS

§6.1. Named tool (one Google Sheet, MFC — contacts, owned by Hunter, shared with nobody), with the reasoning for not using a CRM or Monday at this volume. Fourteen fields plus six further fields listed in §6.2, each with an AG-016 justification written out — including the notes field, whose justification cell had been a literal dash and is now a rule about what may and may not be recorded. The not-collected row is explicit and absolute on wellbeing data. Tab 2 (the room log, no personal data) fixes the defect that a room producing zero contacts was invisible to its own kill criterion. The privacy position is stated once, correctly narrowed — the small-business exemption argument is given as MFC's position with its reasoning, and the "health service" counter-argument sitting in MFC's own insurance declaration is named rather than assumed away. Retention, the deletion trigger and the no-transcription-of-distress rule are added. The contacts-never-go-to-Monday distinction is written down.

T2 · What Hunter looks at monthly — PASS

§6.2. The template exists, on one page, ten minutes to fill, with a plan-says column so every line can be found wrong. Encounters, contacts, conversations, clients, cohorts, revenue, by channel. Two break-detection numbers with their thresholds. A SOURCES block naming which tab each line comes from — including the honest statement that partnership counts come from neither tab. What is deliberately not on the page (followers, likes, impressions, reach, opens, visits) is listed with the reason. The residual — NOTICE cannot separate social from ambassador — is named and resolved by reporting a combined 0.7 line rather than left as a silent error.

7 · WHAT THE HEADS AGREED — H

H1 · Per-head agreement, named — PASS

§7.2. A table with all eight heads the condition names (marketing, commercial, community, social, movement, product, compliance, digital), each with a verdict, a reason in that head's own domain language, and a "what changed because they ran" column. 8 of 8 AGREE, with head-of-movement's recorded as "AGREE on the approach" and a refusal to sign until four defects were fixed — a distinction that matters and was not smoothed over.

It is per-head and it is substantive: every head that agreed also found a further defect while agreeing, and those are named specifically — commercial found channel 4's inputs did not reproduce its own output; product found a fresh ten-second slip propagating through the recomputed cut order and a 5:18 five-minute build; digital re-traced all fourteen monthly-page lines and found six unsourced plus a new false completeness claim; compliance found a second wording of the unit surviving ninety seconds after the verbatim one and the Reel specified as unscripted; movement found a false "APPLIED" status in §7.1 itself. A round of eight yeses finding nothing would have been evidence the question was not real. This one found eleven things.

H2 · The improvement pass, per head — PASS

§7.1. Eight per-head tables, each with numbered findings, an applied/refused status with the section it landed in, and a "biggest thing wrong" row answering Hunter's standing question. Findings changed the artefact rather than confirming it — the unit of sale, the partnerships channel, the covert self-run rep, Option D, the DM trigger rule, the per-channel keywords, the room log. Four refusals are collected in one place with a reason each, including one (MV4) explicitly held rather than dropped, with the condition under which it returns.

One defect, not disqualifying: the header claims "Thirty-eight findings came back. Thirty-four were applied, four were refused." The tables list 43 numbered findings (marketing M1–M6, social S1–S6 and compliance C1–C6 each carry six, not the five their headers claim), and two of the four refusals are "biggest thing wrong" rows rather than numbered findings. The count claim does not reconcile with the tables underneath it — in a document whose own §7.2 celebrates catching a false status claim by grep, that is the same class of error one layer up.

This document. All twenty-six conditions scored against their own pass observables, by a reviewer with no exposure to the build. Arithmetic re-derived independently in G1, G2, E1 (both tables) and the revenue band. E2's rooms counted from the plan's own inclusions and exclusions. Failures named below in priority order.

VERDICT

SHIP — with E2 closed before the first month runs.

Why ship. Twenty-two of twenty-six conditions pass on their own observables, and they pass on substance rather than assertion. The arithmetic that could be checked, checks: the fifteen minutes sum to 15.00 contiguously, the seven-row cut order is correct at every step after three prior attempts were wrong, the five-minute build lands at 5:03, the hours sum to 5.00, and the client chain reproduces end to end at 4.0. The compliance work in A3 is a real call with named conditions and named things it would not license. The heads' round found a founder quote used against himself, a banned claim wording in three places, a false APPLIED status in the plan's own changelog, and six unsourced lines on the page Hunter decides from — and each is visible in the document rather than quietly repaired. The plan also does the harder thing twice: it answers Hunter's social question with the unflattering "no," and it reverses its own first recommendation on the community when the proof underneath it did not hold.

Why not cut. The single FAIL is a count shortfall on a list, not a structural defect. It costs one sourcing hour to close and changes nothing else in the document. Three PARTIALs are each a bounded addition — a tag, a criterion, an enum value — not a rebuild. Cutting a plan this converged over a room count would be disproportionate, and the plan's own first month (rooms 1–4, all already warm) does not depend on the missing three.

The condition on shipping. E2 is the condition the spec singled out in advance as the place node 1 was weak. Node 2 named more rooms and then, correctly, disqualified six of them — good judgement that left the count below the floor and did not go back for replacements. That gap should be closed before Hunter is asked to run the first month, not after, because the year's arithmetic (11 professional and 20 community standings from ~15 yeses) is drawn against a list that no longer holds enough rooms to supply it.

One thing outside the twenty-six, flagged rather than scored. §1.2 correctly names that Hunter is committing five hours a week of lead generation inside the 11.5 MFC-hours a week the money case assumes for delivery, and that no document in the chain shows him the total. That is not this plan's to resolve and it is right not to have resolved it here — but it is the number most likely to be the real constraint, and it should not be lost.

2 · G2 — PARTIAL (both earlier passes scored PASS on "the table sums to 5.00")

This is the contradiction the first review missed and the second inherited. Its verdict cited "the hours table sums to 5.00" as a verification. The plan's own front page, written after that table, says the 5.00 is fiction: ~6.0–7.3 steady state, ~12 in month one, and "the headline total is only true if Hunter declines the recommendation being made to him."

The observable is "a weekly plan Hunter can read in a minute," not "a table that adds up." Scored on the observable:

  • A weekly allocation exists (Mon sourcing 0.75 / room 1.75 / Thu follow-up 0.75 / Fri social 0.5 / partnerships 0.9 / ambassador 0.35) and the flex rule is written in full — up to 8h gated on an issued proposal, capped at six weeks, down to 0.75h when two cohorts are contracted, never from follow-up. That half passes cleanly.
  • But the only weekly plan in the document is for a budget the document disavows. There is no by-week, by-channel allocation of the honest 6.0–7.3 hours, and none at all for month one's ~12 — which §1.3 itself says needs "its own budget outside the flex rule." The +1.30 line is a single aggregate row covering nine named work items; Hunter cannot read his actual week off it.
  • The flex rule as written cannot fund month one. §1.3 says so explicitly: the builds are funded from a ceiling that triggers only once a proposal has been issued, and in month one none exists. A flex rule that is locked in the only month it is needed is a written rule, not a working one.

Ruling: PARTIAL. Both observables are literally present, and the plan is unusually honest about why the table is wrong — but a weekly plan that misstates the week by 20–46% is not the artefact G2 asks for, and scoring it PASS because the cells sum is scoring arithmetic instead of the observable. The fix is one table: the honest week, allocated, and month one allocated separately.

3 · G1 — PARTIAL (unchanged, and for four reasons, not one)

  2. Channel 5's 0.2 clients has no inputs at all. Stated as "the honest number" and derived from nothing. The plan's §7.5 concedes this.
  3. §6.2's "18 conversations/yr" does not reconcile. The chains give 5.0 (prof) + 1.6 (community) + 5.0–6.0 (partner) + 2.5 (social) + ~1.0 (ambassador) ≈ 15–16. Naming a number as wrong is not recomputing it and is not marking it gross. The band's bottom rail is the one Hunter has $12,000 riding on.
  5. 5 · FQ11 — the arithmetic reproduces; the presentation is correct

"~10 cohorts" reproduces from the plan's own chains. 1.0 + 1.2 + 0.3 + 0.5 + 0.2 = 3.2 clients; 13 cohorts ÷ 4.0 clients = 3.25 cohorts per client; 3.2 × 3.25 = 10.4 ≈ 10 ✓. The honest hours reproduce too: 5.00 + 0.50 + 1.30 + 0.50 = 7.30 ✓. Two inputs to that total are soft — channel 5's 0.2 is underived and channel 2's 1.2 should be 1.44 on its own chain — so the honest range is better read as ~3.2–3.4 clients ≈ 10–11 cohorts. That does not change the fork.

It is correctly presented as Hunter's decision. Three real forks, a recommendation with a reason (run at five for one quarter, decide in December on the first real value of X4), conditions 5 and 6 named, and it appears both at the top of the document and at §8 rather than only in an appendix — which is 00-ONBOARDING.md question 5 answered properly. Fork (b) also carries the number nothing else in the chain shows him: a ~19-hour MFC week once delivery is included. ✓
